# Build history

> **Agents: do not read this file.** It is the record of finished work, kept so that
> [BUILD.md](../BUILD.md) can stay short. Nothing in it is needed to do the work: what is
> still to do is in BUILD.md, and every rule or lesson that still applies is in
> [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) and the other files in `docs/`. Open it only when the owner asks
> about the history, or to follow a link that points here, and then search it (`grep`)
> instead of reading it through.

How it is kept: when a session's entry leaves BUILD.md's handoff log, or a finished step or
phase leaves BUILD.md, it is pasted under the marker below, newest first, under a heading
that says when and what, without reading the rest of this file. The text is preserved as
written: boxes and **Remaining** lines are as they stood when the text was moved, and
BUILD.md tracks whatever is still open. Commit hashes refer to `main`.

<!-- newest entries go directly below this line -->

## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-28: a handoff entry

**2026-11-28 · P5D.6: the app's native lobby.** The owner asked for as much of the build as could be done. **Built** (`845858a`): the lobby in SwiftUI from a grown `LobbyModel` (practice per bot with its result, the wallet, the table list with quick seat and filters, sitting down, holds, the waitlist, the new-table form, private tables through the share sheet, invites from a link or pasted), and a stand-in table screen until P5D.7; the lobby's words moved into `web/src/lib/nl/lobby.ts` with a Swift port held by the display fixture. **Found:** P5D.5's CI run failed on iOS 26.5, where a gallery opened while the lobby loaded was popped when the toolbar's link was replaced; fixed in the same commit. **Plan notes:** build 1 is unreleased, so the new requests need no new `AppBuild.number`; the app rejoins the table it last followed at launch, so UI tests leave their tables (AGENTS.md). **Checked:** as in P5D.6's evidence. **Pushed** to GitHub and Codeberg through `f4d7b96` with the owner's approval; its CI run has not been checked. **Next:** P5D.7, with P5D.9 beside it.


## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-28: a handoff entry

**2026-11-28 · P5D.5: the site's look in the iPhone app.** The owner asked for as much of the build as could be done. **Built** (`3736f93`): `pnpm art` writes the app's tokens (from `theme.css` and `kit.css`), every card and sprite as shapes the app draws natively, and its asset catalog (the icon in three styles, the launch colour, the accent); RiverMindKit's kit (cards, buttons, frames, field, felt) and the debug gallery with 29 snapshot-tested items; parity with Chromium's drawing of the site's SVG at @2x and @3x. **Plan notes:** the kit's views live in the package, not the app, so the tests reach them; snapshots are compared only on the macOS they were recorded on (CI's runner is macOS 26 and only checks that each item draws); the drawing tests run one at a time, since together they starved the table connection tests' two-second waits until those crashed. **Checked:** as in P5D.5's evidence; CI run 46108457274 on `8685d8c` passed. **Pushed** to GitHub and Codeberg through `f09c8e7` with the owner's approval; its CI run has not been checked. **Next:** P5D.6, with P5D.9 beside it.

## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-27: P5D.3 and P5D.4, and a handoff entry

- [x] **P5D.3 · The table connection** · Depends on: P5D.2
  - [x] A Swift port of what `connection.svelte.ts` does: hello and welcome, following and leaving tables, acting on the view's `seq` (a stale action refused, never retried blindly), the errors, and rejoining after a drop with the same backoff.
  - [x] It rejoins by itself when the app comes back from the background, when the network moves between Wi-Fi and cellular (`NWPathMonitor`), and when the server restarts mid-hand, with the hand in progress intact. Without voice, the app lets go of the socket when it is suspended (Q-16).
  - [x] A Swift integration test drives a real hand against the seeded server, started as the Playwright tests start it, and receives the events and views the recorded session expects. It shows that an app's seat is marked connected before its first view, as `Tables.join` requires.
  - Evidence: `f8e4bad`. `TableConnection` (`RiverMindKit/Table/`) with `URLSessionTableSocket` and `NetworkWatcher`; 16 unit tests with a fake socket and a manual clock follow `connection.spec.ts` (hello and join, newest view, `whoWas`, one action on the view's `seq`, a refused action asking for `refresh` and never resent, `rate_limited` and `not_seated` asking nothing, backoff 1, 2, 4, 8 s reset by a view, closed and unknown tables, an ended session, leaving, a saved table rejoined, reactions, the held seat taken when ready, the app's phases with and without voice, a network move). Through the real entry points: `TableConnectionSessionTests.swift` played the seeded practice table (seed 10, bust in hand 4, refill, two more hands) over a real `URLSession` socket and matched `session.json` exactly (55 events, 8 turns), which `ios-session.spec.ts` writes from a TypeScript client and checks on two fresh sites, and the guest's seat was connected in its first view; `TableConnectionRestartTests.swift` killed the PostgreSQL-backed server mid-hand (`web/e2e/restart-server.ts`) and the connection rejoined by itself with the hand, board, pot, cards and turn intact, then acted; a UI test pressed Home and brought the app back, and the debug probe showed a second join and the table open. Found: a server started without a fixed secret signs the guest out on restart, which the check first saw as a lost table. The seeded site moved into `web/e2e/seeded-site.ts`. Checks: `ios/test.sh` with the test database, 70 package tests and 4 UI tests; pytest 477; check, lint; Vitest 1,296 with the database; build; Playwright 205 passed and 17 skipped in six projects with the journey (load 29 to 42).
  - Remaining for later steps: a real move between Wi-Fi and cellular needs a real iPhone (P5D.16); the restart check needs PostgreSQL, which CI's macOS runner lacks, so it runs locally.
  - CI: run 46104957715 on `5df65b1` (2026-11-28) passed its `test`, `web` and `ios` jobs.

- [x] **P5D.4 · The page's display logic in Swift, held to it by parity** · Depends on: P5D.2
  - [x] Swift ports of what the app shows that the page computes: `hand-name.ts` (with the browser-safe evaluator in `src/lib/engine`), `bet.ts`, `replay.ts`'s steps, `faces.ts`, and the wording of events, errors and results from `wording.ts` and the table's components, so that the app says what the site says.
  - [x] A script (`pnpm ios:fixtures`) writes fixtures from the TypeScript (seeded random cases, and every hand class), and Swift tests must match them exactly; CI runs both sides. A change to either side lands in both in the same commit, as AGENTS.md asks of the engines.
  - Evidence: `970b673`. `RiverMindKit/Display/` ports `hand-name.ts` with the browser-safe evaluator (Deuces-derived, its notice updated), `bet.ts`, `faces.ts`, `replay.ts`, `safety.ts` and `wording.ts`'s card names; the pots' lines, a seat's status word and its spoken label moved out of `NlTable.svelte` and `NlSeat.svelte` into `bet.ts` and `faces.ts` so both clients read one source. `ios-display.spec.ts` writes `display.json` (2.2 MB): 935 card sets covering every class at every size from two to seven cards with the evaluator's value, 400 bet cases from real decisions, 751 hand-log events (every recorded one and every kind), 106 pot results, 426 seats, the faces, 50 seeded engine hands read back as a viewer's `HandDetail` (1,478 steps; every step kind, side pots, splits, uncalled bets, uncovered stacks, antes, tabled all-ins, mucks, two to nine seats), card names and the safety words. `DisplayParityTests.swift` (10 tests) matches it exactly; a planted change on the Swift side (the C formatter in place of `toFixed`, a misspelt word) failed it, and one in `bet.ts` failed the Vitest staleness check. JavaScript's `toFixed` is ported by its rule (`Faces.toFixed`: 0.25 is 0.3, where `%.1f` gives 0.2). Checks: `ios/test.sh` 52 package tests and 3 UI tests; pytest 477; check, lint; Vitest 1,295 with the database; build; Playwright 205 passed and 17 skipped in six projects with the journey on PostgreSQL (load 20 to 29).
  - CI: run 46104957715 on `5df65b1` (2026-11-28) passed its `test`, `web` and `ios` jobs.

## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-27: a handoff entry

**2026-11-27 · A visual pass over every screen (owner's request; no step).** The owner asked for every screen and view to be reviewed visually and its layout, spacing and look corrected. **Reviewed:** every `pnpm visual` scene (the lobby, watching, the player menu, heads-up at the first turn, a bet being sized, the result, the review, practice, the invite, nine seats, the seat tools, `/style`, `/fairness`, `/hands`) in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit at the five sizes, and by hand `/bots`, the lobby with trained bots, a held seat, table voice's panel and tables of Calling bots, which all bet, at 9, 6, 5, 3 and 2 seats. **Fixed** (`4cc7fc5`): (1) a seat's plate jumped 21px when its player folded and 10px when the turn's clock appeared, on every screen; a seat's box no longer changes size. (2) Nine RiverMind bots all read “RiverMind Eas…” on a desktop seat, and so did the practice opponent; a bot's seat leaves the site's name out (“Easy 4”), as a phone's pod did. (3) On the wide table a bet covered its own seat's “Bot” tag, the end of the board or the next seat's bet; bets now clear seats, tags and the board by pixels (`betSpot`), and another seat's clock is a bar on its plate. (4) At 1024 × 768 and 1280 × 720 a nine-seat ring overlapped the board; the felt takes the height a short screen has. (5) A ten read as a one in every compact hand (“10 7” was “17” in the hand list and the review). (6) Two bet sizes lit at once when they came to the same amount. (7) The player menu and the invite link pushed the table down on a desktop; they lie over it, as on a phone. (8) On a phone the seat tools' edges showed above the action panel, and the dealer button covered a corner pod's stack. (9) The bots page's row headings were bold by accident (`font-normal` makes no CSS on this theme) and its tables' columns did not line up; the hand list cut its titles short on a phone; the seat tools left “React” alone on a row; the lobby's opponent rows were tight. **Closed:** P6.0's leftover about a nine-seat table at 1024 × 768. **Not done:** a phone held sideways and a window under about 650px tall still put the dock over the table (P6.0d), and a phone's zone under the table is empty for someone watching. **Checked:** pytest 477; check, lint and build; Vitest 1,291 with the database; Playwright 205 passed and 17 skipped in the six projects with the journey on PostgreSQL; `pnpm visual` before and after in three browsers at one density, where `/style` and `/fairness` are pixel for pixel the same and every table, lobby and hands scene differs as meant. AGENTS.md and [docs/design.md](docs/design.md#the-table) hold the rules. **Pushed** to GitHub and Codeberg through `491a707` with the owner's approval; its CI run has not been checked. **Deployed** with the owner's approval: `update.sh` passed on the host (pytest 474 passed and 3 skipped, Vitest 1,290 passed and 1 skipped, the candidate and the voice candidate, 13 tables restored), release `20261128T011845Z-491a707b1171`, `/healthz` and `web-live` report `491a707`, the voice service was left running from `bcbf4c9`'s release, and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 21 with 3 skipped (the restart test and the three-browser voice test, as unset). **Next:** as before, P5D.3 or P5D.4.

## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-27: P5D.2

- [x] **P5D.2 · Protocol v2 and the API in Swift** · Depends on: P5D.1
  - [x] A generator (`pnpm protocol:swift`) writes the Swift `Codable` types from `web/src/lib/protocol-v2.ts`, and the voice signalling types from `web/src/lib/voice/signalling.ts`, into the package. CI fails when the committed Swift is stale, as it does for `pnpm protocol`.
  - [x] Every server message recorded in `table-contract.json` and every HTTP body in `contract.json` decodes in Swift and encodes back to the same JSON, compared parsed and never as text (JavaScript writes `20` where Python wrote `20.0`).
  - [x] An app reading a newer server does not break: unknown fields are ignored, and an unknown event kind, error code, reaction or bot kind becomes a value the app can show generically. A fixture with such additions proves it.
  - [x] An API client covers every route the app uses. It keeps the session cookie in the Keychain, makes or renews the guest with `POST /api/session`, and sends no `Origin`, which the server already accepts from a client that is not a browser (`originAllowed`); verify each route against a local server, and that the app's requests stay within the API's and Caddy's rate limits.
  - Evidence: `69cc617`. `pnpm protocol:swift` (`web/scripts/protocol-swift.ts`, TypeScript's parser) writes `ProtocolV2.swift` and `VoiceSignalling.swift`; `protocol-swift.spec.ts` failed on a planted line and passes on the written files. `pnpm ios:fixtures` writes `ios/RiverMindKit/Fixtures/protocol.json` from both recorded sessions (553 server messages, 26 client messages, 82 response and 16 request bodies; the table session replayed against its digests), and `ios-fixtures.spec.ts` failed on a planted edit. Swift decodes every one and encodes it back to the same JSON, compared parsed; `table_closed`, which no recording has, is a hand-written case. Newer-server tests: added fields, an unknown event kind, error code, reaction, bot kind, suit, message type and voice notice all read. Found: the server writes an optional field as null in some messages and leaves it out in others, and a new table's missing `bots` (the default bot) differs from null (no bots), so such fields keep a public `nulls` set. `APIClient` covers every route in docs/ios.md, keeps `rm_session` in the Keychain, makes the guest again on a 401 and reports a 429 without retrying; a walk through every route against a local `pnpm start` stayed under 30 requests with no 429, and the same session was the same guest. In the Simulator (iOS 27.2) a UI test relaunched the app and found the same guest, and after `simctl uninstall` and `install` it was still the same guest. Checks: `ios/test.sh` 42 package tests and 3 UI tests; pytest 477; `pnpm check`, `pnpm lint`; Vitest 1,304 with the database tests; build; `pnpm e2e` 223 passed and 17 skipped with the journey on PostgreSQL (load 20 to 29).
  - CI: run 46085279025 on `1d2915d` passed (2026-11-27), its `test`, `web` and `ios` jobs.

## Moved from BUILD.md on 2026-11-27: a handoff entry

**2026-11-27 · P5D.2: protocol v2, voice signalling and the API in Swift.** The owner asked for as much of the build as could be done, then to stop after P5D.2. **Closed P5D.1:** CI run 46078754689 passed its `ios` job on Xcode 26.6, so P5D.1 moved to the history. **Built** (`69cc617`): `pnpm protocol:swift`, which writes the app's protocol types from `protocol-v2.ts` and `signalling.ts` (open string types, message enums with an unknown case, constants for literal fields, `JSONValue` for mediasoup's parameters), with hand-written voice envelopes; `pnpm ios:fixtures` and the Swift round-trip tests over every message and body of the two recorded sessions; tests that a newer server's additions still read; `APIClient` for every route, the session in the Keychain, a 401 answered by a new guest and a 429 never retried; the placeholder lobby shows the guest. The `--seats` reports say what it does there, and `/bots` says the gate's evaluation is at six. (2) A smaller-table report has no format, so it can never be gated or recorded in the registry. (3) Medium plays six seats only where the six-seat champion's own Medium is recorded, so each new champion needs its Medium evaluated before Medium follows it; the 1 billion champion's was never recorded. (4) A six-seat bundle that fails its check leaves both bots at two seats and fails the candidate's `champions` check, so a deploy would stop before the switch rather than serve half a site. The figures are on P5.7's **Deployed** line. No release tag was made: none was asked for. Waiting on the owner: the word on nine seats' option 1 before any run; whether Phase 6 may start before Phase 5B closes.

### Phase 5C · Two looks: clean and pixel

**Starts now, ahead of Phase 6** (D-18, owner, 2026-11-27). The site gets a second look, clean and professional with no pixel art, beside the pixel-art look it has today. Players switch between them at any time, the choice is kept in their browser, and clean becomes the default once it is finished. Both looks share one set of pages, components, markup and behaviour: a look is a stylesheet, a set of art and a deck, chosen by `data-look` on `<html>`. So the two cannot drift apart in what they do, and every test can run in either. Until P5C.7 the default stays `pixel`, so commits deployed for other work never show players a half-drawn look.

**How it is built.** `web/src/lib/look.svelte.ts` holds the look and keeps it in `localStorage` (`rivermind.look`). Everything that belongs to the pixel look (the kit in `pixel.css`, the wallpaper, the pixel font, pixelated rendering, card snapping, stepped animations) is scoped to `:where([data-look='pixel'])`, which adds no specificity, so the pixel look stays exactly as it was. The clean look redefines the theme's variables under `[data-look='clean']` and gives every kit class its own drawing in `clean.css`. Its art is hand-drawn SVG, written by a generator beside the pixel one and checked the same way. The cards are FeltPrep's code-drawn deck, ported onto RiverMind's 71 × 95 card box so every slot, fan and peek keeps its geometry.

**Exit criteria**

- [x] Both looks are live, the clean look is the default, and a player can switch from any page and from the table on a phone, with the choice kept across reloads.
- [x] Every Playwright test passes in both looks in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit at desktop and phone sizes, and P6.0c's phone-screen measurements hold in both.
- [x] The pixel look is unchanged from before the phase, pixel for pixel, apart from the switch itself (`pnpm visual:compare`).
- [x] Nothing pixel is left in the clean look: no pixel font, wallpaper, nine-slice frame, sprite or stepped animation.
- Exit checked on 2026-11-27: the first criterion by `look.live.ts` on the live site (P5C.7's **Deployed** line); the second by CI run 46069180920 and the local runs, `table.e2e.ts`'s phone measurements among them; the third by `pnpm visual:compare` at each step (P5C.1 identical to `e09ef41`; P5C.2 to P5C.6 differing only at the switch, the header band and a phone's table bar; P5C.7 identical to `b02d303`); the fourth by the full-resolution reviews of P5C.5 and P5C.6 and `clean.spec.ts`, which fails on a pixel sprite, a stepped or moving loop, or a colour off the clean palette.

### Phase 5B · Multi-seat bots and difficulty levels

**Starts after P5.9 and the Phase 4C exit criteria** (D-15), both met since 2026-11-22. Extend the training and evaluation pipeline one format at a time.

**Exit criteria**

- [x] Heads-up, 6-max and 9-max each have a promoted champion, a published evaluation meeting P5.5, and the difficulty levels in P5.7.
- [x] Each format meets the serving latency and memory budgets on ubuntu-vm.

  - [x] Extend the design estimates and seat-rotated harness to 6-max, then 9-max. For each, train, evaluate and promote a champion under P5.5. Record compute used and the results. If a format falls short, record what was tried, the measured gap and what it needs, and leave that format open.
  - Evidence (the six-seat design and code; no blueprint trained): `1d58ad5`. [docs/bots.md](bots.md#6-max-and-9-max) has the format `6max-s1`, why its betting states are merged, the candidates counted, the graphs' sizes and SHA-256s, the probe's speed and memory and the estimate for the run. Built: an `Abstraction` per format; a multiway compiler whose merged graphs are 345,581 and 654,597 decision states at 50 and 100 big blinds (8,465,576 nodes at six seats when every history has its own, for a smaller size table); a walk for any number of seats that on two seats makes the heads-up walk's updates exactly (checked on the `hu-m1` trees and tables over six chunks with pruning, and in a test on stand-in cards); `MultiwayBlueprintPolicy` for three to six players; a full-table duplicate match with results by position; format 2 serving bundles; and the `c200` card tables (200 buckets a street, built in 181 s from `3888572`'s card code). One probe through the CLI before the hold: 2,560,000 iterations on ten workers at 23,000 to 24,000 a second with the load average at 35 to 47, 14.4 GB summed over processes, a 5.3 GB checkpoint. Checks: [test_blueprint_multiway.py](../test/engine/test_blueprint_multiway.py) (21 tests: the graphs against the design, merging against the unmerged tree, payouts against the engine with real cards, the two walks' agreement, worker-count independence and exact resume, a learning check, seats by position, blueprints staying on the graph at three to six dealt in, mixed tables with short and deep stacks, a format 2 bundle rebuilt and hashed from its columns, and the harness); full pytest 460. The `hu-m1` trees hash as before and the existing blueprint, match, bundle and registry tests pass unchanged. No web files changed.
  - Evidence (the second table build, the pruning probe and the start of the run, 2026-11-22): `c200` built a second time from `ca7965d` into a scratch directory with six workers in 218 s (2.1 GB peak summed over processes, at a load average of 40 to 90): all 15 data files are byte-identical to `artifacts/tables/c200` (built from `3888572` with ten), and only the manifest's build and software sections differ. The pruning probe ([how](bots.md#training-six-seats)): a scratch run of 24,000,000 iterations with the run's schedule on eight workers (19,175 a second, 12.9 GB peak summed over processes), then its regrets scaled to their expected size at 80 million, 400 million, 1.8 billion and 3.6 billion iterations and 16,000 iterations played with pruning in every iteration at nine thresholds. Unpruned, an iteration visited 39.6 decision nodes; `hu-m1`'s −10,000,000 would cut that to 16.0 at mid-run (60%), where heads-up's choice cut 31%, and −60,000,000 cuts it to 39.6, 35.9, 27.1 and 22.7 at the four points (0, 9, 32 and 43%). Chosen: **−60,000,000, with the floor at −66,000,000**. The run `artifacts/blueprints/6max-s1-s0` started at 18:05 with those settings (**Background run** in **Now**).

Its interval holds halfway between Easy and Hard against all five opponents ([the table](bots.md#difficulty-levels-p57); Easy loses −11.62 to five copies of this champion). **At smaller tables**, the served bundle with `blueprint evaluate --seats`, against RiverMind Easy and calling stations at 100 big blinds: three seats +24.00 and +282.84, four +32.91 and +788.36, five +22.82 and +807.22, every interval above zero ([docs/bots.md](bots.md#serving-six-seats-p57)). **On ubuntu-vm** both champions' bundles were benchmarked in a transient unit with the web unit's sandbox and its new limits, 3 GB and 4 GB (load average 0.9). Over 20,000 decisions at three to six dealt in, the six-seat champion took 0.005 ms at the median, 0.012 ms at p95 and at most 0.74 ms. The heads-up one took 0.009 ms at p95. Memory was 1,661 MB resident, and the unit peaked at 1.9 GB including page cache, under `MemoryHigh`. **Through the real entry point** (rebuilt with the new registry): the server checked both bundles (the six-seat one in 0.7 s) and logged “RiverMind Medium plays half its hands as hu-m1-s0-1024m (or 6max-s1-s0-3600m with three or more dealt in)” and “RiverMind Hard plays tables of 2 to 6 seats: hu-m1-s0-1024m with two dealt in, 6max-s1-s0-3600m with three or more”. `GET /api/config` listed Medium and Hard at 2 to 6 seats with both models. In the browser pane, a six-seat table against five RiverMind Medium bots played 16 hands (the guest checking or calling every turn) with no error in the page or the server log. **Checks:** pytest 469, check, lint, Vitest with the database 1,271, the three parity runs, the build and `pnpm e2e --workers 6` with the database (198 passed, 12 skipped).
  - Deployed (six seats, 2026-11-23 local time): the owner approved the commit, push and deploy. `713f093` was pushed to both remotes and deployed as release `20261124T020259Z-713f093c5c64`. `update.sh` passed 466 Python tests (3 skipped) and Vitest 1,270 (1 skipped) on the host, verified all five registered champions, had no migration to run, and passed both candidate checks. It left the voice service running unchanged from `bcbf4c9`'s release and restored 10 tables. The service logged both bundles checked against the registry (the six-seat one in 1.8 s) and “RiverMind Hard plays tables of 2 to 6 seats: hu-m1-s0-1024m with two dealt in, 6max-s1-s0-3600m with three or more”. `/healthz` and `web-live` report `713f093`. The unit runs with `MemoryHigh` 3 GB and `MemoryMax` 4 GB, and used 1.6 GB after the restart. `GET /api/config` lists Medium and Hard at 2 to 6 seats with both models. `pnpm e2e:live` passed 19 with 3 skipped, and failed `champion.live.ts` in two projects: the test still expected Hard at two seats only. It was fixed in the commit that records this line to expect each model's sizes and to find every served champion on `/bots`, and then passed in both projects against the live site. **By hand** in the browser pane, a private six-seat table against five RiverMind Hard bots dealt 8 hands on the live site with no error, until the guest, checking or calling every turn, went bust.
  - CI: run 45308917037 on `713f093` passed both jobs: pytest 466 (3 skipped), Vitest 1,270 (1 skipped), the parity runs, Playwright 196 (14 skipped) with the six-seat tests, and `pnpm voice:check` 60 with 27 skipped.
  - Evidence (nine seats, built and tested; not deployed, 2026-11-24): `ed4820c` (another session) serves a nine-seat model beside the six-seat champion it builds on: `TablePolicy` gives a hand with seven to nine dealt in to `nine.ts`, which `pnpm bots:parity` checks against Python; `5de896f` leaves out a model built on an older six-seat champion with a warning. **Medium at nine seats** was evaluated by that session over 20,000 deals a matchup at `e5e7353` with a clean tree, and recorded with `blueprint level` by the commit that records this line: +4.78 (+1.49 to +8.07) against eight RiverMind Easy, +1,125.80 against eight calling stations and −3.12 (−9.61 to +3.38) against eight RiverMind Hard at 100 big blinds, its interval holding halfway between Easy and Hard against all five opponents ([the table](bots.md#the-first-nine-seat-champion-2026-11-24)). **Through the real entry point:** the built Node server with `RIVERMIND_MODELS=../artifacts/models` checked all three models against the registry and logged “RiverMind Hard plays tables of 2 to 9 seats: hu-m1-s0-1024m with two dealt in, 6max-s1-s0-3600m with three or more, 9max-o1-s0 with seven or more” and Medium likewise; `GET /api/config` listed Medium and Hard at 2 to 9 seats with the three models; in the browser pane the lobby offered both at nine seats (“2 to 9 seats.”), and a nine-seat table against eight RiverMind Hard bots dealt three hands with no error in the page or the server log, until the guest, checking or calling every turn, went bust.
  - Evidence (decision time and memory for every format, 2026-11-24): on ubuntu-vm, `pnpm bots:bench` from `aa7f15c`'s sources with all three models from `/srv/rivermind-poker/models/champions` held at once, in a transient unit with the web unit's sandbox and limits (3 GB `MemoryHigh`, 4 GB `MemoryMax`), load average 0.2. p95 decision time, without the table's 0.6 s thinking delay: heads-up 0.009 ms, three to six dealt in 0.012 ms, seven to nine 0.019 ms (at most 2.3 ms in any format, over 20,000 decisions each). Memory: 1,612 MB resident, 1,351 MB of it array buffers; the unit's peak, page cache included, 2.6 GB, under `MemoryHigh`. The live unit serving heads-up and six seats peaked at 1.6 GB over its first 20 hours. [docs/bots.md](bots.md#the-first-nine-seat-champion-2026-11-24) has the table.

The service logged “RiverMind Hard plays tables of 2 to 9 seats: hu-m1-s0-1024m with two dealt in, 6max-s1-s0-3600m with three or more, 9max-o1-s0 with seven or more” and Medium likewise. `/healthz` and `web-live` report `2a3de6d`; `deploy/smoke.sh https://rivermindpoker.com` passed; `GET /api/config` lists Medium and Hard at 2 to 9 seats with the three models; `pnpm e2e:live` passed 21 with 3 skipped (the restart tests, without a restart command) at a load average near 4 on this Mac. **By hand** in the browser pane, a private nine-seat table against eight RiverMind Hard bots on the live site dealt five hands with no error while the guest checked or called every turn, and `/bots` describes `9max-o1-s0` with no sideways scroll. The unit, holding all three models, used 1.8 GB and peaked at 2.0 GB of its 3 GB `MemoryHigh` in its first minutes, with nothing at warning level in its log.

### Phase 5A · Practice, heads-up opponent and replay

**Starts after Phase 4.** P6.0b shipped before the stack change; P5.5 onward starts after Phase 4B (D-14). Phase 4C (table voice) runs alongside and goes first when both phases have a ready step (D-15). The player milestones below are in execution order. The bot work between practice and replay is limited to what it takes to qualify and serve one heads-up opponent. Multi-seat training, additional difficulty levels and shareable replays have their own later steps.

#### 1. Start a practice hand in one click

- [x] **P6.0b · One-click practice at any balance** · Depends on: P3.7, P4.2, P4.5, P4.6
  - [x] A prominent “Practice vs bots” action starts a heads-up hand against the labelled RiverMind Easy bot from the lobby. Seats, blinds and a practice stack have defaults, so the first hand takes one click for a new guest or a guest at zero chips before their next top-up is due.
  - [x] Each guest can resume one active practice table after reload. Only that guest can access it. Concurrent start requests return the same table, and abandoned tables expire under the server’s existing capacity and rate limits.
  - [x] A player who busts can start another hand with a fresh practice stack. Practice chips stay at the table. Practice hands appear in “Your hands” with a practice label. On phone and desktop, verify the first hand, reload and playing again after a bust. Server tests check that practice leaves the wallet and ledger unchanged, marks stored hands for exclusion from competitive stats and refuses access to a second guest.
  - Evidence: `99c08ea`. `POST /api/practice` seats the guest at a private two-seat table (50/100) against RiverMind Easy with `--practice_stack` chips (10,000 by default), or returns their table; per-guest locks make concurrent starts return one table. Practice tables pay nothing to or from wallets, are kept out of the lobby, quick seat and seat or waitlist requests, refill a busted guest with `rebuy`, close after an hour unwatched when the server needs room, and mark `poker_tables.practice` (migration `0006`), so hands carry `practice`. Checks: [test_practice.py](test/server/test_practice.py) (8 tests) and the restart test in [test/db/test_hands.py](test/db/test_hands.py); [practice.e2e.ts](../web/e2e/practice.e2e.ts) in all six browser projects (new guest: first hand, reload to the same table, bust, “Play again”, labelled hands, wallet unchanged; zero-balance guest); full pytest 557 of 558 with the database (the random-play coverage check missed a `hold` attempt at load average 27, then passed 3 of 3 alone), Vitest 64, `pnpm e2e` 102 of 102. In the browser pane at phone width: one click, reload, bust, “Play again” and the hands page, wallet 20,000 with only the grant.

#### 2. Play one evaluated heads-up opponent

- [x] **P5.1 · Abstraction design** · Depends on: P6.0b, P2.7
  - [x] `docs/bots.md` defines the bot design:
    - the action abstraction for each street (for example fold, check or call, ½ pot, pot, 2× pot and all-in);
    - how off-tree bets map to the abstraction;
    - the card abstraction: 169 lossless pre-flop classes, and post-flop buckets clustered on hand-strength distributions;
    - the info-set key format;
    - memory and training-time estimates for heads-up on this Mac (Q-5). Add estimates for 6-max and 9-max at P5.6.
  - Evidence: `919ba16`. [docs/bots.md](bots.md) fixes `hu-m1`: heads-up without an ante at 50 and 100 big blind depth classes, three or fewer sizes a node plus all-in, pseudo-harmonic mapping with re-entry, 169 lossless classes and 1,000 equity-distribution buckets a street, and text and slot keys. Throwaway scripts outside the repo (described in the doc) built three candidate trees through the engine (3,772, 11,708 and 52,084 decision nodes at 100 bb) and timed external-sampling MCCFR on the compiled medium tree: 21,107 iterations/s on one core and 113,567 on ten. They also counted the suit classes by Burnside’s lemma (169, 1,286,792, 13,960,050 and 123,156,254) and timed river equity at 0.51 ms a board, giving 352 MB of training tables and about 9 hours of heads-up training, with card tables under 2 hours. The worked examples (the key’s history, 250 open, 375 bet, 1,900 pot raise, *p* = 0.533) were replayed on the engine. pytest 558 passed with the database tests.

- [x] **P5.2 · 52-card clustering pipeline** · Depends on: P5.1
  - [x] The clustering is generalized to the full deck. Simulations and K-means are seeded so table generation is reproducible. Each table set has a manifest with its settings, version and SHA-256.
  - [x] Generation time and peak memory are measured and recorded.
  - Evidence: `401b49e`. [rivermind/bots/cards](../rivermind/bots/cards/) and `rivermind-poker tables build`, `verify` and `bucket` implement the [docs/bots.md](bots.md#card-abstraction) card abstraction with exact enumeration (no simulation) and their own seeded k-means; scikit-learn's parallel k-means changed centroids between runs. Three full builds (10 and 6 workers before the commit, 10 from it) gave byte-identical files, 421 MB in 16 files. The canonical set `artifacts/tables/hu-m1` was built from `401b49e` in 193 s with a peak of 3.2 GB summed over processes; the six-worker build took 200 s and 2.3 GB. Its manifest, hashes and timings are in [the table sets](bots.md#table-sets). All 1,000 buckets are used on each street, and bucket order tracks mean equity (rank correlation 0.988 on the flop and 0.994 on the turn, over 400 random hands). Lookups give the same bucket under every suit relabelling and take about 5 µs. [test_card_tables.py](../test/engine/test_card_tables.py) checks the class counts against Burnside, river equity against brute-force enumeration, the histograms against direct recomputation, seeded clustering, and a limited build through the CLI (reproducible, verified, tamper and stray-file detection, refusal to overwrite). pytest 570 passed with the database tests.

- [x] **P5.3 · Heads-up training** · Depends on: P5.2, P2.6
  - [x] Monte Carlo CFR runs on the new engine with linear discounting and pruning, using several cores. It keeps the RM-02 and RM-04 guarantees: exact resume, consistent checkpoints and atomic writes.
  - [x] Checkpoints carry a manifest: engine version, abstraction and table hashes, settings and iteration count.
  - Evidence: `b6fa49f`, `d2dd215`. [rivermind/bots/blueprint](../rivermind/bots/blueprint/) and `rivermind-poker blueprint` (`tree`, `train`, `info`, `strategy`) are described in [docs/bots.md](bots.md#training-p53). The trees compiled from the engine match P5.1 exactly (11,708 and 5,472 decision nodes, 43,937,574 slots). On Kuhn poker the trainer reaches an exploitability of 0.011 with seat 0's value within 0.005 of −1/18. Through the CLI on the hu-m1 tables, 768,000 iterations with discounting and pruning gave byte-identical `regrets.npy` and `strategy.npy` from 10 workers straight through and from 6 workers stopped by Ctrl-C at 448,000 and resumed on 3. A probe on a 25.7-million-iteration run set the pruning threshold at −10,000,000, since Pluribus's −300,000,000 would prune nothing in a run this size. Ten workers reached 66,000 to 71,000 iterations/s from uniform strategies and 30,000 to 40,000 once trained play visited 86 decision nodes an iteration, not 46, using about 6 GB. [test_blueprint.py](../test/engine/test_blueprint.py) covers trees, sizes, legality, Kuhn convergence with and without pruning, worker-count independence, exact resume, discounting, atomic and hashed checkpoints, refused mismatches, the run lock and the CLI on the real tables. pytest 587 passed with the database tests.

- [x] **P5.4 · Evaluation v2** · Depends on: P5.3
  - [x] A duplicate, seat-rotated match harness evaluates the same deals from both seats against random, calling-station, heuristic (RiverMind Easy) and, once available, current-champion opponents. Report big blinds per 100 hands and a 95% interval for each matchup. Compute uncertainty over independent deals, keeping both seat rotations together. Further variance reduction (for example AIVAT) is optional.
  - [x] Save reports under `docs/evaluations/`. Record rules, seats, stacks, blinds, opponent versions, seed, deal and hand counts, commands, settings and artifact hashes. Rerunning those inputs reproduces the reported results exactly.
  - [x] For each candidate, also report results at 40, 100 and 150 big blinds effective, against a probe that min-bets every street, and the share of decisions that found trained data ([docs/bots.md](bots.md#mapping-real-actions-onto-the-tree)).
  - Evidence: `0c31c60`, `565daef`, `f362a5e`. [`BlueprintPolicy`](../rivermind/bots/blueprint/policy.py) plays a checkpoint through a `SeatView` with the [mapping](bots.md#mapping-real-actions-onto-the-tree), and [rivermind/bots/match.py](../rivermind/bots/match.py) with `rivermind-poker blueprint evaluate` plays duplicate matches and writes [docs/evaluations](evaluations/) ([method](bots.md#evaluation-p54)). The first candidate, `hu-m1-s0` at 128,000,000 iterations, has its report in [hu-m1-s0-128m](evaluations/hu-m1-s0-128m.md). Over 20,000 deals per matchup it won +61.1 bb/100 (+50.9 to +71.4) against RiverMind Easy and +308.3 (+289.4 to +327.3) against the calling station at 100 big blinds. Against Easy it won +43.5 at 40 big blinds and +74.3 at 150, and against the min-bet probe +392.4. Its own info set had trained data in 99.9% or more of its decisions. Rerunning it on 4 workers instead of 10 gave the same JSON apart from `--out`, and on the exploratory checkpoint 3 and 8 workers gave byte-identical reports. [test_match.py](../test/engine/test_match.py) covers the mapping (the design's *p* = 0.533, an off-tree open drawn 58% to the lower size, below-smallest bets, all-ins, re-entry, untrained nodes, real-pot sizing at other stakes and depths), the probe, exact zero for two calling stations, reproducibility across workers and the gate. pytest 597 passed with the database tests.

- [x] **P5.5 · Promotion gate and model registry** · Depends on: P5.4
  - [x] Promote the first champion for a format when the lower bound of its 95% interval is above zero against **each** of RiverMind Easy and the calling station. Replacements must also clear that threshold against the incumbent. The gate must hold for the checkpoint and for the serving bundle exported from it, whose rounded strategy is what the site plays. Record failed or inconclusive runs and leave promotion open. The RM-06 short-deck result applies only to the prototype.
  - [x] `docs/models.md` lists each promoted champion by format with checkpoint and table hashes, settings and evaluation links.
  - [x] Artifacts stay out of Git. Their storage and backup location on ubuntu-vm is documented.
  - Evidence: `2ea0f41`, `5d14f5b`. `rivermind-poker blueprint promote` ([registry.py](../rivermind/bots/registry.py), [how it works](bots.md#promotion-and-the-registry-p55)) checks the checkpoint, the bundle and both reports against each other, records the decision in [docs/models.json](models.json) whatever the outcome, regenerates [docs/models.md](models.md) and keeps a promoted run in `artifacts/models/<name>/`; `blueprint evaluate` now takes a bundle. Run for real on 2026-11-21 over 20,000 duplicate deals a matchup: `hu-m1-s0-128m` became the first heads-up champion (its served bundle +60.74 bb/100, +50.53 to +70.96, against RiverMind Easy and +307.13 against the calling station), and `hu-m1-s0-1024m` replaced it, beating it by +21.38 (+10.71 to +32.05) as checkpoints and +21.14 (+10.45 to +31.84) as bundles while clearing the gate against both bots. Both are on ubuntu-vm in `/srv/rivermind-poker/models/champions/`, each of their 15 registered files matching the registry by `sha256sum`; [where models are kept](bots.md#where-models-are-kept) covers both machines and the backup. [test_registry.py](../test/engine/test_registry.py) covers promotion, failed and inconclusive runs, replacement, refused inputs, the kept files and a bundle as candidate and opponent, and replays the published decisions from their reports. pytest 626 passed with the database tests; no web files changed.

- [x] **P5.7a · Serve the evaluated heads-up opponent** · Depends on: P5.5, P4B.6, P4B.7
  - [x] A guest can choose RiverMind Easy or the promoted bot for a two-seat table, including in practice. Keep the one-click Easy default and let a guest switch opponents between practice hands. Play a complete hand against each and verify the Node server loaded the serving bundle exported from the champion’s registered checkpoint, with matching tables (P4B.6).
  - [x] Label the bot and link its selection to the evaluation with the game settings, opponents, number of hands, win rate in big blinds per 100 hands and 95% intervals. Record p95 decision time under 200 ms and peak service memory within the unit limit on ubuntu-vm. Measure decision time separately from the short presentation delay. Document how to deploy a champion under D-9.
  - Evidence: `2416fc0`. With `RIVERMIND_MODELS` the Node server plays the registry’s heads-up champion as **RiverMind Hard**, after [`loadChampion`](../web/src/lib/server/bots/champions.ts) has checked every file of its bundle, its card table set and its checkpoint against [`champions.json`](../web/src/lib/champions.json), the registry extract `blueprint promote` writes ([how it works](../CONTRIBUTING.md#rivermind-hard-serving-a-champion-p57a)). It is offered for two seats only; “Practice vs bots” still starts RiverMind Easy in one click; `POST /api/practice {"bot"}` chooses the opponent and changes it between hands, from the lobby and from the practice table’s “Opponent” control; and its label links to `/bots`, which shows every matchup of the published evaluations. A saved table whose bot a server does not run plays on against RiverMind Easy, in both servers, so a rollback keeps seats and chips. Verified with the registered bundle `hu-m1-s0-1024m`: in the browser against the built server on this Mac (the log names the bundle’s `strategy.u8` and table set; hands against each bot, a change asked mid-hand and made when the hand ended), and on ubuntu-vm, where the same build in a transient unit with the web unit’s sandbox and limits loaded the champion from `/srv/rivermind-poker/models/champions` in 0.7 s and played 3,100 hands at 100 concurrent practice tables in 12 s with no error and a memory peak of 844 MB against the 2 GB `MemoryHigh`. `pnpm bots:bench` there: decisions at 0.005 ms median, 0.014 ms p95 and 0.034 ms p99, apart from the table’s 0.6 s thinking delay ([docs/bots.md](bots.md#serving-p4b6)). Checks: pytest with the database 629; check, lint, Vitest with the database 661, the three parity runs and the build; `pnpm e2e --workers 2` 126 of 126 in all six projects ([champion.e2e.ts](../web/e2e/champion.e2e.ts) is the new part) and the `E2E_LEGACY` pass 18 of 18 with the 3 champion tests skipped; `pnpm visual` against the commit before it: 396 of 462 captures identical, and the other 66 are the two intended changes (the lobby’s new “The bots” link, and the new avatar on `/style`).
  - CI: run 44917209265 on `e6f261a` passed both jobs: pytest 627 (2 skipped), Vitest 666 (the registered bundle’s test skipped, as it is not in CI), the parity runs, Playwright 126 of 126 and the `E2E_LEGACY` pass 18 of 18.
  - Deployed: `e6f261a`, release `20261121T150841Z-e6f261adab0a`, on 2026-11-21 with the owner’s approval. `update.sh` passed 627 server tests (2 skipped) and Vitest 666, verified the champion’s files against the registry, ran no migration and restored 6 tables; the service logged “RiverMind Hard plays hu-m1-s0-1024m … checked against the registry in 0.6 s”. `pnpm e2e:live` passed 16 of 16 on Chromium desktop and WebKit phone, with the 2 restart tests skipped (no `LIVE_RESTART_COMMAND`): its new test practised against RiverMind Hard on the live site, changed to RiverMind Easy between hands and opened the evaluation on `/bots`. The service’s `MemoryPeak` after those runs was 545 MB of its 2 GB `MemoryHigh` (518 MB at start-up; 50 MB before the champion), with no error in its log.

#### 3. Review the hand and return to the table

- [x] **P6.1 · Replay from the table** · Depends on: P5.7a, P3.5, P4.6
  - [x] “Review hand” on the settled result opens that exact hand. The player can also browse and open older hands from “Your hands”. Step forward and back through the recorded actions with the board, cards, acting seat, amounts bet or called, and pot sizes as of each action.
  - [x] Explain why each pot was awarded. For a showdown, name the winning hand when its cards were shown (for example, “king-high straight”). For a fold-out, identify the last player standing. Verify a showdown, a fold-out and a side pot against the stored results. The response contains only cards the viewer is entitled to see, and the replay reveals them at the recorded street or showdown.
  - [x] On phone and desktop, review a settled practice hand, return to the same table and act in the next hand. If the next deal begins during review, the live clock and action controls remain reachable. Verify this also works at a wallet-backed table.
  - Evidence: `7324f9e` (the model), `1fa63f0`. [`HandReplay.svelte`](../web/src/lib/nl/HandReplay.svelte) steps through [`buildReplay`](../web/src/lib/nl/replay.ts)'s steps with First, Back, Next and Last or the arrow keys, opening on the result: the board, the pot, each seat's cards (the viewer's throughout, anyone else's face down until turned over), bets, the acting seat and, on the result, each seat's net and a sentence for each pot. `/hands` opens any of the player's hands in it. At the table, “Review hand” beside a settled result opens that hand, found in `GET /api/hands` by table and hand number, and “Review last hand” in the hand log's heading opens the newest; the replay takes the hand log's place beside the table, or sits above the table on an upright screen, with the turn and its clock named in its heading, so the action panel stays in view and the hand in progress can be played under it ([how it works](../CONTRIBUTING.md#no-limit-table-server)). No server or protocol change. The model gained one step: hands turned face up when the betting ends all-in (rule 12.1), before the rest of the board, where it used to show them only at the showdown. Checks: [`replay.spec.ts`](../web/src/lib/server/api/replay.spec.ts) (a showdown, a fold-out, a side pot, a split and an all-in run-out played by the engine, against the stored pots, shares and nets, and what each viewer may see at each step) and [`replay-steps.spec.ts`](../web/src/lib/nl/replay-steps.spec.ts); [`replay.e2e.ts`](../web/e2e/replay.e2e.ts) in all six browser projects, at a practice table and a wallet-backed one (review the last hand against the stored hand, step by button and key, act with the review open and play on under it, close, review the settled hand from its result, sit back in and act), 72 of 72 over six repeats, and [`hands.e2e.ts`](../web/e2e/hands.e2e.ts) (stepping, and a second hand opened over the first). Full runs at load 35 to 45 with training in the background: pytest 438; check, lint, Vitest with the database 669; `pnpm e2e --workers 4` 138 of 138. `pnpm visual` against three captures of `e2f4b8b`: 165 of 561 identical (the lobby, watching, the player menu, `/style`, `/fairness`), and the other 396 are the intended changes (the hand log's new button at every table scene, “Review hand” at the result, the `/hands` copy and replay, and the three new scenes). Looked at in Chromium at 375×812, 768×1024, 1024×768 and 1440×900 against the dev and the built server.
  - Deployed: `58c9c0b`, release `20261121T181312Z-58c9c0bc74eb`, on 2026-11-21 with the owner's approval. `pnpm e2e:live` passed 18 of 18 on Chromium desktop and WebKit phone (the 2 restart tests skipped), its journey reviewing a hand from the table there.

#### Phase 5A exit criteria

- [x] **P5.9 · Verify the three priorities together** · Depends on: P6.0b, P5.7a, P6.1 · **Owner gate** for publishing, pushing and deploying under D-9
  - [x] In Playwright against the real Node server and a disposable PostgreSQL database, cover a fresh guest and a zero-balance guest before top-up. Start practice in one click, choose the evaluated heads-up opponent, complete a hand, open that hand from the result, return and play again. Verify wallet and ledger totals before and after.

### Phase 4C · Table voice

**Goal.** The people seated at a table talk to each other live, as they would at a real one, with end-to-end encryption, low delay and high-quality audio (D-15). It is the site’s headline feature. Muting yourself, muting one player and muting everyone each take one click. [docs/voice.md](voice.md) holds the design: the threat model, mediasoup as the forwarding server, SFrame (RFC 9605) frames keyed from an MLS group (RFC 9420), the audio settings and what the host must provide.

**Starts now** (owner, 2026-11-20) for P4C.1 to P4C.4, which touch no table code. They add new files under `web/src/lib/voice/`, `web/src/lib/server/voice/` and `web/voice/`, and stay out of the files Phase 4B steps own. P4C.5 onward builds on the TypeScript table server (P4B.5b), and voice goes live only after the cutover (P4B.7), so none of it is built twice. Phase 5A runs alongside: the two share no files. When a session can take either a ready Phase 4C step or a ready Phase 5A step, it takes the Phase 4C step. Phase 5B starts only once both P5.9 and this phase’s exit criteria pass.

**Ground rules**
- **End to end or not at all.** No code path sends audio the voice server could decrypt. If a browser lacks `RTCRtpScriptTransform`, or the group key cannot be agreed, voice does not start and the table says why. There is no unencrypted fallback.
- **Nothing kept.** No recording, transcription or stored audio, and no stored keys. The voice service keeps room state in memory only and logs counts, durations and errors, never handles, addresses or tokens.
- **Consent first.** The microphone is requested only after the person chooses to join voice. While it is live, the table always shows it.
- **Measured, not assumed.** Delay, quality under loss, bandwidth, CPU and memory are measured and recorded with the setup used, as the engines and bots are.
- **Formats stay put.** Table protocol v2 and the HTTP contract do not change for voice (P4B wire compatibility). Voice has its own signalling protocol with a version field from its first message.

**Exit criteria**
- [x] On the live site, three or more people at one table, on Chromium, Firefox and Safari across phone and desktop, join voice, talk and use the controls, with the table voice code matching on every screen. (P4C.9 evidence. Reworded under D-16: the phone is Safari 27.2 in the iOS Simulator, which joined, listened and used mute, deafen and the panel but was heard by nobody, its microphone being silent there; Playwright's WebKit at a phone's size spoke and was heard; every control is exercised in all three engines by `voice.e2e.ts`. A real iPhone and an Android phone are P7.1b.)
- [x] Tests show the voice server forwards only frames it cannot decrypt, a player who left cannot decrypt what is said after, and a newcomer cannot decrypt what was said before. (P4C.2, P4C.3 and P4C.8 evidence)
- [x] Mouth-to-ear delay, quality under packet loss and the host’s voice capacity meet P4C.7’s targets, measured locally and live. (P4C.7 and P4C.9 evidence. Live means the live service from inside the home network, where the path added about a millisecond and lost no packet, and the host's own capacity figures; delay between two networks is P7.1b's, under D-16.)

- [x] **P4C.3 · Group key agreement with MLS** · Depends on: P4C.2
  - [x] ts-mls is pinned, and the MLS working group’s interoperability test vectors that apply to ciphersuite 0x0001 pass in CI. (They run in the Vitest step; GitHub’s own first run of it is P4C.2a’s.)
  - [x] The group works as [docs/voice.md](voice.md) describes: the first member creates it, later members join by external commit, the voice service as external sender proposes removals, the remaining member with the lowest leaf index commits them, and the service accepts only the first valid commit for an epoch. Vitest drives two to nine members through random joins, leaves, reconnects and simultaneous commits, and checks that every member ends on the same epoch with the same exporter output and that a removed member’s state cannot derive the new keys.
  - [x] SFrame keys come from each epoch as RFC 9605 section 5.2 specifies, with `E` and `S` recorded. Receivers keep the previous epoch for a grace window. With a continuous tone playing, a join or a leave costs the listeners no more than one 20 ms frame of the decoded tone.
  - [x] The table voice code, four words from the epoch authenticator, is the same for every member of a group and changes with every epoch. A client whose group holds a member who is not seated at the table warns and stops sending audio until the group matches the seats. (At P4C.3 the client reports the strangers to its caller, `Epoch.seats`; the words on the table are P4C.6’s. Someone the page saw seated has three seconds after leaving their seat before they count: their removal takes a commit, and stopping at once silenced every speaker each time anyone stood up.)

[`group.spec.ts`](../web/src/lib/voice/group.spec.ts) (27 tests) drives [`Member`](../web/src/lib/voice/group.ts) and the service’s [`Delivery`](../web/src/lib/voice/delivery.ts) through joins, leaves, reloads, simultaneous joins and commits, quiet committers and a seeded random walk of 120 steps at up to nine seats, with everyone on one epoch, base key and authenticator after each and every departed page locked out, and through everything a member and the service refuse. [`group-keys.spec.ts`](../web/src/lib/voice/group-keys.spec.ts) (16 tests) seals and opens frames with real key stores across epochs (`E` = 4, `S` = 6), the grace window, the wait for a late key, the voice code and the seat check. `pnpm voice:check`, 39 of 39 on this Mac and 30 with 9 skipped in Playwright’s Linux browsers: a real call keyed from the group through a join and a leave refused and dropped no frame in any engine and concealed 0 samples in 15 of 15 runs at the pages’ own pace (0 to 39 ms with the listener 60 ms behind), and the code is the same words in Chromium, Firefox, WebKit and Node. New sprites are drawn from palette names in `web/art/` and rendered by `pnpm art` (docs/design.md).

The worklet and the `.wasm` file are bundled same-origin files, and the page CSP gains only `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` in `script-src`. A recorded voice over a recorded room (keyboard, fan, chatter) measurably loses more noise than with the browser’s own suppression alone; record the figures, the added delay (about 20 ms) and the CPU. (Reworded under D-16: it adds 20.7 ms, which stands, and its CPU is recorded for this Mac, 0.5 to 1.1% of a core; a phone's is P7.1b's.)

### Phase 4B · TypeScript stack migration

**Goal.** Run the live site on the D-14 stack: SvelteKit 3, Svelte 5, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS 4, PostgreSQL 18 with Drizzle ORM, and Node through `adapter-node`, behind Caddy. Players see no change except a short reconnect at the cutover. Python keeps only the offline work: the reference engine, card tables, training and evaluation.

**Starts now** (owner, 2026-11-20). It comes before P5.5. Blueprint training (the `hu-m1-s0` run) keeps going on this Mac and is not affected. Until P4B.7 the live site stays on `serve-nl`, and fixes to it stay in Python.

**Ground rules**
- Wire compatibility. Protocol v2, the HTTP API, session cookies (HMAC-SHA256 over the same fields and secret), table snapshots and hand histories keep their formats, so the existing client, open tables, stored hands and guests’ cookies survive the cutover unchanged. Any format change gets a version bump and a reader for the old version.
- The Python engine is the reference. The TypeScript engine must produce byte-identical hand histories for the same deals and actions, and CI checks this on every push.
- Port tests along with code. A Python test that guards a behaviour gets a Vitest or Playwright counterpart before the Python code is removed. The Playwright suite is the end-to-end parity check.

**Exit criteria**
- [x] The live site runs on the Node service. Caddy proxies to it, and nothing in the serving path imports Python. (P4B.7 evidence)
- [x] The engine and bot parity checks pass in CI, and the web suite (check, lint, Vitest, build, Playwright in three browsers) passes against the Node server with a real PostgreSQL. (P4B.4, P4B.6 and P4B.7 evidence: in CI run 44876245683 Vitest's database project runs the table server on PostgreSQL 18 and Playwright's three browsers run it with tables in memory; `pnpm e2e:live` runs both browsers against the live server on PostgreSQL.)

- [x] **P4B.1 · Node server on `adapter-node`** · Depends on: none · **Owner gate:** deploy
  - [x] `web/` builds with `@sveltejs/adapter-node`. A custom server (for example `web/server/index.ts`, compiled into the build) mounts the adapter’s `handler.js` and a `ws` WebSocket server on `/ws` on one `http` server. It honours `PORT` and `HOST` itself, answers `/healthz`, and on SIGTERM stops taking sockets, lets in-flight changes finish and then exits.
  - [x] Until P4B.7, setting `RIVERMIND_LEGACY_API` (for example `http://127.0.0.1:4316`) forwards `/api/*` and `/ws` to `serve-nl`, so the app runs against the Python server in development, in Playwright and in CI.
  - [x] The table and lobby stay client-rendered (`ssr = false` on their routes). `kit.csp` in hash mode, or SvelteKit’s nonces, keeps the no-inline-code CSP true for server-rendered pages, and `pnpm build` still fails on inline script or style.
  - [x] Node is pinned to one LTS major in `web/.node-version` and `engines`. `pnpm check && pnpm lint && pnpm test && pnpm build && pnpm e2e` pass in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit against the Node server forwarding to `serve-nl`. CONTRIBUTING.md documents the new commands.
  - [x] Added in this step, because the static deploy cannot serve a Node build. `deploy/update.sh` publishes each build as a release that `rivermind-poker-web.service` runs on 127.0.0.1:4320. Caddy sends pages and assets there and `/api/*` and `/ws` straight to `serve-nl`, and keeps the pages’ own CSP. The pieces are written and checked; deploying them is the owner gate, after which `pnpm e2e:live` must pass.
  - Evidence: P4B.1 commit (see the handoff log). `web/server/` (`app.ts`, `legacy.ts`, `index.ts`) is bundled with `ws` into `build/start.js` by `vite.server.config.ts`. Vitest has 13 tests for the server and CSP helper (forwarding with Host, Origin, cookies and X-Forwarded-For, 503 while `serve-nl` is down, the spliced socket, 1013 without a legacy server, draining on close). `pnpm build` runs `scripts/check-inline-code.js`, which fetched `/`, `/fairness`, `/hands` and `/style` from the built handler and failed as intended on a test inline `<script>` and a test style attribute. Check, lint, Vitest (77), build and Playwright passed: 102 tests in six projects, plus the new `site.e2e.ts` (headers, hashed CSP, immutable caching, 404s, `/healthz`) in all six. The Caddy site validated against the host’s Caddyfile on ubuntu-vm in a temporary copy. Run locally with Caddy, Node and `serve-nl`, pages kept Node’s hashed CSP, the API got Caddy’s, `/ws` reached `serve-nl`, a stopped Node gave the 503 text, and a hand was played in the browser pane with no console errors.
  - Deployed with the owner’s approval: `7ba1952`, then the fix `2a5dc1c`, release `20261120T190332Z-2a5dc1c4c3c4`. `update.sh` passed 596 server tests and restored 9 tables. The first deploy’s `pnpm e2e:live` failed the Chromium practice reload: Caddy’s `?Content-Security-Policy` fallback added its stricter policy to Node’s 304, and Chromium applied it to the cached page, blocking the start-up script. The fix sets Caddy’s policy only on `/api/*`, `/ws` and its 503 page. The old Caddy file failed that test against a local Caddy, Node and `serve-nl`, and the new one passed the whole live suite there. After the fix, the live 304 carries no policy, and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 with the 2 restart tests skipped.

On ubuntu-vm (D-10), the updated `setup-database.sh` added the owner's `drizzle` schema to `rivermind_poker`; a `pg_restore` copy of the live database (178 players, 449 hands, 155 tables, 474 ledger entries) was migrated by the bundled script with `SET ROLE rivermind_poker_owner` and `search_path = app`, a second run found nothing to do, its full `pg_dump` outside `drizzle` was byte-identical before and after, and the journal table belonged to the owner; the copy was then dropped. Vitest has 6 database tests (`migrate.db.spec.ts`, its own `db` project, one file at a time) and 4 unit tests of the name guard and the migration files. In a scratch clone of `main` with these changes: check, lint, Vitest (87, with the database), build, Playwright (108 in six projects) and pytest with the database (598 passed, the 2 card-table tests skipped).
  - CI: pushed `ea1ffa6` with the owner’s approval. In run 44806749771 the Python job passed, including `test_drizzle_baseline.py`, and the `web` job ran `migrate.db.spec.ts` against its `postgres:18` service (6 tests passed, none skipped) and passed check, lint, Vitest (87) and the build. - [x] **P4B.6 · Bot serving in TypeScript** · Depends on: P4B.4
  - [x] The random, calling-station and RiverMind Easy bots are ported. On the fuzz deals, each picks the same actions as its Python counterpart for the same seeds.
  - [x] `rivermind-poker blueprint export <checkpoint>` writes a versioned serving bundle: the `uint8` average strategy, the tree layout and the card-table arrays in flat little-endian files, with a manifest naming the checkpoint, the table set and every file’s SHA-256. The Node server memory-maps or reads it once and refuses a bundle whose hashes or table set do not match.
  - [x] A TypeScript `BlueprintPolicy` (the action mapping, re-entry and nearest-bucket fallback from [docs/bots.md](bots.md#mapping-real-actions-onto-the-tree)) gives the same action probabilities as the Python one, to within 1e-9, on at least 100,000 recorded decisions from `hu-m1-s0`. Card-bucket lookups agree on every hole-card pair for a seeded sample of boards on each street. Decision time p95 under 200 ms is measured on this Mac and recorded; P5.7a measures it on ubuntu-vm.
  - CI: run [44826474817](https://github.com/dunamismax/rivermind-poker/actions/runs/44826474817) on `6cd2cd9` passed both jobs: `pnpm bots:parity` matched 2,000 simple-bot hands, 2,001 blueprint decisions (largest difference 0) and 200 boards a street on the synthetic bundle; pytest 613 passed (2 skipped without the card tables), Playwright 107 passed and 1 flaky (see **Blockers**).
  - Evidence: `b07b8c1`; design and measurements in [docs/bots.md](bots.md#serving-p4b6). `web/src/lib/server/bots/` has `PythonRandom` (CPython's MT19937, seeding and methods), the four simple bots, the abstraction, a tree compiler (Vitest checks both trees against the Python SHA-256s), `loadBundle` and `BlueprintPolicy`; `rivermind/bots/blueprint/bundle.py` exports and reads bundles, and `rivermind.bots.differential` writes the reference for `pnpm bots:parity` (a CI step on a synthetic bundle). `blueprint export` of `hu-m1-s0` at 128 million iterations took 12 s (450 MB, `strategy.u8` `ec57ac6f…`, table set `4ff8b0ef…`, in `artifacts/bundles/hu-m1-s0-128m`). The long parity run (`--hands 2500 --decisions 100000 --boards 1000 --tables artifacts/tables/hu-m1`) matched 20,000 simple-bot hands at 2 to 9 seats byte for byte, 100,002 blueprint decisions against all four simple bots and itself with identical probabilities (largest difference 0), statistics and hand records, and every pair's bucket on 1,000 boards a street against the original `.npy` tables. `pnpm bots:bench` on this Mac (load average about 40): p50 0.006 ms, p95 0.04 ms, p99 0.46 ms over 20,003 decisions, bundle loaded and hashed in 1 to 2 s, 443 MB of array buffers. `loadBundle` refused a changed, missing or extra file, a changed tree and another table set in Vitest; `ServingBundle` and `blueprint export` refused the same in pytest (`test/engine/test_bundle.py`, a fake table set and checkpoint). Fixed in both languages: the policy compared the tree's player (0 is the button) with the real seat, so a bot in seat 1 on the button was lost every decision; P5.4's matches always put the button in seat 0, so its reports stand. The new seat test failed before the fix.
