## Phase 4B session handoffs

**2026-11-21 · The host's web platform contract, and P4B.8: the Python table server and the rollback retired.** The owner adopted one standard for every web project on ubuntu-vm (`ubuntu-vm-backup/docs/web-platform.md`: pnpm and Node 26 at exact versions, exact dependency pins at a shared baseline, the host PostgreSQL cluster, no rollback, a candidate check before traffic) and asked for this repository to follow it, retiring the rollback now rather than after P4B.8's week. (1) P4B.8, except its docs criterion (see its **Remaining** line): the Python server, `rivermind/db`, Alembic, `serve-nl`, `moderation` and SQLAlchemy, Alembic and psycopg are gone; the Python table model stays because `pnpm table:parity` compares the TypeScript table with it, and FastAPI and Uvicorn stay for the legacy `serve`. The Node server's forwarding to `serve-nl`, `E2E_LEGACY` and its CI pass, the serve-nl handover test, `deploy/rollback/` and `deploy/rollback-to-serve-nl.sh` went too, and the Python CI job no longer needs PostgreSQL. (2) Pins: every web dependency exact, at the baseline where it names one (adapter-node 6.0.5, kit 3.4.0, svelte 5.58.1 and vitest 5.1.1 were the only changes; the others were already at the version the lock had), Node `26.13.1` in `.node-version` and `engines`, `saveExact` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, and Python `3.12.15` in `.python-version`, which uv installs from `uv.lock` with `--locked` everywhere. (3) The deploy (`deploy/update.sh`) follows the contract's flow: build and test from `git archive` outside the lock, then one `with-deploy-lock` process that stages the release with `REVISION`, dumps and migrates with the bundled `build/migrate.js` as the service's user, checks a candidate, switches, restarts, verifies with `/healthz` and `web-live`, and applies Caddy through `caddy-apply`. The candidate is the new part: `RIVERMIND_CANDIDATE=1` starts the release beside the live server on the real database and checks the database, that every saved table can be restored (`Tables.restorable`) and the champion, but never restores, deals or saves a table, which two servers running the same tables would. `/healthz` now answers JSON with the revision and a database check; `deploy/smoke.sh` is the smoke check for the candidate, the live port and the public site. The unit runs `/opt/node-26/bin/node`, reads `/etc/web-apps/rivermind_poker/web.env` and keeps its dynamic user and sandbox. Release directories keep their `<time>-<commit12>` names because the host backup's `config/applications.json` expects them. Checks before committing, on ubuntu-vm: `bin/web-conformance` passes; pytest 436 passed and 2 skipped on 3.12.15; check, lint, Vitest with the database 660 passed and 1 skipped (including a new test that the candidate check leaves saved tables untouched and fails on one this build cannot restore), `pnpm engine:parity`, `pnpm bots:parity`, `pnpm table:parity` (100 sequences, 25,000 requests) and the build; the bundled migrator and a candidate on the test database. Playwright was not run (no browsers on the host; CI runs it). Deployed with the new `update.sh`: the first run (`1154b38`) stopped at its frozen install because that commit carried the lockfile from before the pins, with nothing on the host changed; `ceb612a` commits the right lockfile and deployed twice, the second time to prove the restart. Both runs passed the candidate on port 4400, restored every open table (7, then 8), reported `ceb612a` on `/opt/node-v26.13.1-linux-x64/bin/node` through `/healthz` and `web-live`, applied the Caddy fragment through `caddy-apply` and passed the public smoke check. The proof: a guest on a live practice table held its own turn on the flop of hand 48 (pot 400, facing a 200 bet) while the second deploy switched; the restart closed its socket with 1012, and 1.4 s later the new release served the same hand, board, hole cards, stacks, bets and legal actions; the guest then played it out and it was stored as hand 9019. On the host: the env files moved to `/etc/web-apps/rivermind_poker/` (with `DB_OWNER_ROLE` added) and `/etc/rivermind-poker` is gone; `rivermind-poker.service`, the `rivermind-poker` account and its ACL on `/home/sawyer` are removed; `/var/lib/rivermind-poker` is root's, with the session secret and serve-nl's old SQLite file (`tables.sqlite3`, last written 2026-11-19), which is left for the owner to delete. Next action: P6.1, as before; P4B.8's docs line when convenient.

**2026-11-21 · P4B.2 and P4B.7 complete: Tailwind, and the live cutover to the Node table server.** The owner approved, for this session, pushing `main` when the suites pass, deploying after each push, and the cutover once P4B.2 was done and every P4B.7 criterion met. In order. (1) Pushed P4B.5b. CI failed twice in Vitest's database project, both times in the tests' own waits, which counted turns of the event loop: an idle loop on CI spends 20,000 turns in milliseconds while PostgreSQL saves are in flight, and this Mac under load hides it. All three waits in `tables.testing.ts` now also last at least ten seconds (`c28cef2`, `ecab6fa`); run 44868353525 passed and `ecab6fa` was deployed (14 of 14 live). (2) Built the screenshot comparison before touching a style: `pnpm visual` and `pnpm visual:compare`, with a seeded test-only server in `web/e2e/visual/` (CONTRIBUTING describes it). Making it repeatable took four fixes: reload at the first turn (the hand log holds what the page happened to be sent while joining), park the pointer (Firefox drew a button hovered or not), scroll to the top (a sticky dock is drawn where the scroll position puts it), and several baseline runs (Chromium anti-aliases rotated cards differently between runs at DPR 3). (3) P4B.2 in two commits, each matching all 462 captures: `9e73932` puts Tailwind 4.3.5 in the build with the theme `pnpm art` generates, and `26be9fa` moves every page and component to utilities; `22843f8` then names the tokens on `/style` and in docs/design.md, the one intended difference. Split the step that way, as the owner suggested, so a preflight problem could not hide behind a conversion mistake; both kinds turned up and each was found in its own stage. Reworded P4B.2's second and third criteria (the kit was never scoped CSS and stays as the kit; the short-deck components were left alone; `/style` gaining token names is the difference the second criterion itself asks for). (4) P4B.7: `7764859` has the unit, the Caddy site, `update.sh` and a rollback script; rehearsed both directions mid-hand on a copy of the live database, then cut over at 06:50 UTC. Everything is in the P4B.2 and P4B.7 evidence. Checks before each commit, at load 60 to 240: check, lint, Vitest with the database 648 of 648, the three parity runs, build, pytest with the database 618 of 618, Playwright 108 of 108 (stage two's full run failed the practice bust test once on phone-sized Chromium, then passed 20 of 20; see **Blockers**), and for the last tree the `E2E_LEGACY` pass 18 of 18. `22843f8` and `7764859` were tested together as one tree. Pitfalls, now in AGENTS.md: a second Playwright run in `web/` empties the first one's `test-results`; never put two utilities for one property on an element (the stylesheet's order decides, not the markup's: a tag with `ml-3` and `ml-0` kept `ml-3`); scoped CSS is unlayered and beats every utility; a variable named `state` in a component turns `$state` into a store read; `grid-cols-5` is `minmax(0, 1fr)`, not `1fr`; write a shadow or a translucent colour as an arbitrary value when it must match exactly. Decisions for the owner: none blocking. Two to know about: Tailwind's fixed overhead is about 9 KB, and the built CSS still came out 2.1% smaller only because utilities replaced more than that; and P4B.8's week runs from 2026-11-21. Next action: P5.5, or P4C.1 in parallel; P4B.8 from 2026-11-28.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.5b complete: the table server in TypeScript.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible, and for improvements along the way; took P4B.5b, the last big step before the cutover. `web/src/lib/server/table/` ports `table.py`, `clock.py`, `snapshot.py`, `runner.py` and `store.py` (on Drizzle); `api/tables.ts` and `socket.ts` port the rest of `Server` and the `/ws` endpoint; `api.ts` gains the lobby, seating, quick-seat, practice, report and relation routes; `web/server/app.ts` serves `/ws` (403 for another origin, 64 KB frames, 1012 on shutdown); `runtime.ts` restores saved tables before the server listens and reads `RIVERMIND_HAND_PAUSE`, `RIVERMIND_BOT_DELAY` and `RIVERMIND_MAX_TABLES`. Parity is measured three ways, each described in CONTRIBUTING: a table fuzz by digest (`pnpm table:parity`, `parity.json`), a 168-step session recorded from `serve-nl` on one event loop (`table-contract.json`), and a database test in which a real `serve-nl` and this server take over each other's tables, which is the P4B.7 cutover and its rollback. The recorded session matched for its first 44 steps at the first run; the four differences it then found were each fixed or made deliberate. Deliberate differences from `serve-nl`: bots decide on the event loop and each bot turn first yields to it; the runner wakes its loop only after a change is saved and published, so event order no longer depends on save latency (without this the PostgreSQL replay ordered a bot's action ahead of a disconnect); a refused action's message says what is allowed instead of quoting the engine's `repr`. Improvements beyond the port: `Site` gives its in-memory wallet the site clock; `Tables.close` (close a table now, paying back whoever is seated) and `onTable` (hear a table from its first change, which Phase 4C will want) are public; Playwright runs against the Node server's own tables by default, with `E2E_LEGACY=1` for `serve-nl`; CI gains `pnpm table:parity` and a Chromium pass in front of `serve-nl`; the live restart test no longer trips on a seat's “Away” label; README, CONTRIBUTING and AGENTS describe the new server. Changed the plan: reworded three P4B.5b criteria (the fairness page reads nothing from the server; the transcript is recorded by a new exporter because `test_protocol_v2.py`'s runs depend on thread timing; the restart tests are the live suite's), and added a P4B.7 criterion (the fairness page and CONTRIBUTING name `crypto.randomInt`, and `update.sh` runs `pnpm table:parity`). Pitfalls: a recording must only act once the server has settled; `uv run` leaves its Python child running unless the whole process group is killed (two orphaned `serve-nl` processes answered on a test port until they were found); a probe that blocks its own event loop with `execSync` cannot answer a WebSocket close, which looked like a 10-second shutdown stall and was not; JavaScript writes `20` where Python writes `20.0`, so socket messages are compared by canonical digest. The Mac restarted mid-session (about 22:28): the tree was intact, the lost Playwright run was repeated, and the blueprint run was resumed from its 512 million checkpoint. Checks are in the P4B.5b evidence. Not pushed, not deployed; the live site is unchanged. Next action: with the owner's approval, push `main` and watch CI (the new `table:parity`, handover and legacy steps run there for the first time); then P4B.2.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.5a complete: sessions, wallets and safety records in TypeScript.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took P4B.5a, the next step on the cutover's critical path, while the `hu-m1-s0` run kept training (402 million iterations at 18:55, checkpoint at 384 million). `web/src/lib/server/api/` ports everything in `serve-nl` that needs no running table: session cookies and legacy tokens, players and handles, wallets and the ledger, rate limits and the handle filter, reports, relations and signals as stores (each in memory and on Drizzle), hand-history views, and request validation with Pydantic's rules and wording, with each schema type-checked against the generated `protocol-v2.ts`. Its routes are config, session, wallet, hands, a hand, and listing and removing mutes and blocks. Split the step: the lobby, seating, practice, reactions, reports, new mutes and blocks, the signal check, the TypeScript-sourced protocol and the socket-transcript contract all act on a running table, so they moved to P4B.5b's new first criterion, unchanged. Reworded the first criterion: the API runs in the Node server ahead of SvelteKit (`web/server/api.ts`), not as `+server.ts`. Through the built server, SvelteKit 3's CSRF check, which cannot be turned off, refused an Origin-less bodiless `POST /api/session` that `serve-nl` accepts, and it would refuse loopback dev pages the same way. The API is opt-in with `RIVERMIND_API=1` until the cutover, because the live web unit sets no `RIVERMIND_LEGACY_API` (Caddy sends `/api` to `serve-nl`). Added a P4B.7 criterion: the web unit's dynamic user cannot read the root-only session secret, so it needs `LoadCredential=`. Parity is measured, not assumed. `test/server/export_http_contract.py` records `serve-nl`'s answers and pure-function values into `contract.json` (pytest checks it is current; Prettier must ignore it), Vitest replays them byte for byte in memory and on PostgreSQL, and side by side on one database the two servers accepted each other's cookies and gave byte-identical hand histories over rows Python wrote. All 33 contract cases matched on the first run. Pitfalls: read stored times as `extract(epoch …)::float8`, because a `Date` drops PostgreSQL's microseconds; JSON.parse cannot tell `6.0` from `6`, so `strictJson` uses the reviver's source text to refuse it as Pydantic's strict integers do; FastAPI reports a body that is not JSON as `body.<offset>`. Checks, at load 28 to 44: pytest with the database 616 passed; check, lint, Vitest with the database 478 passed (`art.spec.ts`'s rendering test needs `--testTimeout` above 5 s at this load, as before); build; `pnpm e2e --workers 2` 108 of 108 in all six projects. With the owner's approval, pushed and deployed `09a95a0` (release `20261121T001710Z-09a95a00b40d`, 614 server tests, 10 tables restored), and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 of 14 with the 2 restart tests skipped. Next action: P4B.5b (or P4B.2 in a parallel session).

**2026-11-20 · Docs: README, AGENTS and the top of BUILD rewritten.** The owner asked for the three files to be made ideal for this repository, using the README, BUILD and AGENTS files of two of their other projects as references. README now leads with what the site is, a status section (Phase 4B, what is live, the first heads-up candidate’s evaluation with its link and the note that it is not yet promoted), the principles from D-1, D-2, D-7, D-8, D-12 and Q-6, a roadmap table, a target-versus-live stack table, the repository layout, local play in the browser and the terminal, and the existing origins and license text unchanged, plus a line on Q-8. AGENTS is reorganized into numbered sections (workflow, autonomy and owner gates, git, parallel sessions and the training run, stack and layout, commands, conventions, testing pitfalls, the live server, security, boundaries, documentation, toolchain, Context7); every earlier rule is kept, and a few pitfalls from recent handoffs are promoted (the bundle reader’s own `ArrayBuffer`, `drizzle-kit pull`, `ORIGIN`, the WebKit trace screencast, the manual clock and a bot’s thread, not calling a load failure flaky unchecked). In BUILD, **Now** keeps only the current state (history stays in the evidence lines and this log) and gains a **Background run** row; **How to work this file** gains the step format, the ticking rule and where new work and ideas go. Checks: every command, flag and path named in the new text was checked against the CLI help, `web/package.json`, the workflow and the tree; the relative links and heading anchors in all three files resolve; the training run was confirmed running (364.7 million iterations at 17:43). Docs only: pytest with the database tests passed (615); no web files changed. Not pushed. Next action: P4B.5a or P4B.2.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.6 complete: the bots in TypeScript.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took P4B.6 (bot serving), the next step that unblocks the cutover, while the `hu-m1-s0` run kept training (336 million iterations at 16:28). `web/src/lib/server/bots/` ports `rivermind/bots`: `PythonRandom` (CPython's MT19937 with its seeding, `random`, `getrandbits`, `randint`, `choice`, which matched CPython on the first try), the random, calling-station, RiverMind Easy and min-bet bots, the hu-m1 abstraction with exact fractions, a tree compiler, `loadBundle` and `BlueprintPolicy`. `rivermind-poker blueprint export` writes a serving bundle (`rivermind/bots/blueprint/bundle.py`: `uint8` strategy, `trees.json`, flat card-table arrays, a hashed manifest with a `table_set` digest), `ServingBundle` reads it back for Python, and `rivermind.bots.differential` with `pnpm bots:parity` checks both languages on the same deals and seeds (a CI step, on a synthetic bundle when no real one is given); `pnpm bots:bench` times decisions. Found and fixed a mapping bug in both languages: the policy compared the tree's player (seat 0 is the button) with the real seat, so a blueprint in seat 1 holding the button was "lost" and checked or called every decision; P5.4's matches always put the button in seat 0, so the reports stand, but live tables alternate it. It also now measures stacks and the opponent's bet over the two players dealt in. Exported `hu-m1-s0` at 128 million iterations to `artifacts/bundles/hu-m1-s0-128m`. Results are in the P4B.6 evidence and [docs/bots.md](docs/bots.md#serving-p4b6). Pitfalls: Node's type stripping rejects parameter properties, as AGENTS.md says; `pnpm format` is the quickest way to satisfy `pnpm lint`; and the bundle reader allocates its own `ArrayBuffer` per file, since `readFileSync` may hand back a pooled buffer whose offset breaks `Int32Array` views. Checks, with the training run and another project's pipeline keeping the load average between 30 and 120: pytest with the database 615 passed; check, lint, Vitest (306: 305 passed with the database; `art.spec.ts`'s two rendering tests hit the 5 s timeout at load 120, and they fail the same way with this session's tests removed), `pnpm engine:parity`, `pnpm bots:parity`, build; `pnpm e2e --workers 2` 108 of 108 in all six projects. Then fixed the `test_practice.py` failure from CI on `36201aa`: a guest seated over HTTP without a socket counts as connected, so a hand that ends without them timing out resets their absences, and the test moved the manual clock 60 s while the bot's thread was still deciding, which timed the bot out and made it fold as the small blind. Under load that used up the test's 50 steps. Each step now waits for the guest's own turn, so the run is fixed by its seeds: 21 steps and 41 hands, the same in 8 of 8 runs (it varied before); `test/server` 169 passed. The server was right: a real guest who leaves disconnects and is away every hand. With the owner's approval, pushed `b07b8c1` and `6cd2cd9` (with `7151de0`) to both remotes; CI run 44826474817 passed both jobs and the first `pnpm bots:parity` in CI (details in the P4B.6 evidence). The owner then approved pushing `a56b278` (this record) and deploying: `update.sh` fast-forwarded ubuntu-vm from `e6098a4`, passed 613 server tests (2 skipped), ran no migrations, restored 10 tables and published release `20261120T222942Z-a56b278de279`; `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 of 14 against https://rivermindpoker.com (Chromium desktop and WebKit phone), with the 2 restart tests skipped (no `LIVE_RESTART_COMMAND`). Play is unchanged: nothing on the site uses the TypeScript bots yet. Next action: P4B.5a or P4B.2. P5.5 should confirm the gate on the bundle's `uint8` rounding.

**2026-11-20 · CI: the WebKit disconnect test, P4B.4 ticked, `36201aa` deployed.** Asked to find why WebKit desktop’s “two people and a bot play on through a disconnect” fails in CI and fix it. It was not the Node server’s `/ws` forwarding: the test has failed in CI since P4.4 added it, before P4B.1. Two causes, both in the test setup. (1) Playwright’s trace (`retain-on-failure` records every test) streams a screencast, and in WebKit that made every action slow: in CI’s trace each click took about a second and often hit `playUntil`’s 1 s limit, hands took about 16 s, and the test used 49 of its 60 s before the reconnect. Locally, six of these tests at once in WebKit took 47 to 51 s with tracing (the same load with ten failed every time), 13 to 15 s without it, 13 to 14 s with `screenshots: false`, and 31 s or a failure with only the DOM snapshots off. The trace now keeps its DOM snapshots and drops the screencast. (2) `playUntil` called `isEnabled()` without a timeout. When a turn ended between `isVisible()` and `isEnabled()`, it waited for that player’s next turn, which came only after the other player, whose loop had already stopped, ran out their 20 s clock and 30 s time bank. That is the bimodal Chromium time in CI (about 9 s or 58 s) and, in run 44813845079, chromium’s “sits out, comes back, rebuys and leaves”, whose trace shows `Is enabled` hanging 58.8 s after the player sat out. It now has a 1 s limit, like the click. Under the same local load, WebKit took 18 to 21 s, Chromium 10 to 13 s in 12 of 12 (2 or 3 of every 6 had stalled at about 59 s), and WebKit phone 15 to 19 s. The `.meta` wait in the CI error was only the call in flight when the test timed out; both pages showed hand 4. Also raised the `web` job’s limit from 30 to 45 minutes: `ea1ffa6`’s run spent 29 minutes fetching Playwright’s Ubuntu packages from a slow mirror, which caching the browsers would not avoid. The wallet-fix session pushed and deployed `36201aa` (with P4B.3’s tick and P4B.4): `deploy/update.sh` ran 607 server tests (2 skipped), no migrations, restored 9 tables, release `20261120T204610Z-36201aa5d395`. Its CI run passed `pnpm engine:parity` and Vitest, so P4B.4 is ticked; its `test` job failed once in `test_practice.py` (see **Blockers**). Checks, with the owner’s machine under heavy CPU and GPU load: pytest with the database 609 passed; check, lint, Vitest with the database 287 and build passed; `pnpm e2e --workers 2` (as in CI) 108 of 108 in all six projects, the disconnect test 9.0 s in WebKit. With the owner’s approval, pushed and deployed `e6098a4`: `update.sh` ran 607 server tests (2 skipped), no migrations, restored 11 tables, release `20261120T211022Z-e6098a406ece`; `pnpm e2e:live` then passed 14 of 14 against https://rivermindpoker.com (Chromium desktop and WebKit phone), with the 2 restart tests skipped (no `LIVE_RESTART_COMMAND`). CI run 44817436740 on `e6098a4` passed both jobs: pytest 607 (2 skipped), Vitest 287, parity, and Playwright 108 of 108 with no retries; the disconnect test took 6.5 s in Chromium but 47.6 s in WebKit desktop (29.9 s on the phone size), close to its 60 s limit. The owner then asked for headroom: the test now sets a 180 s limit, matching its own 60 s and 90 s waits for play; it passed in all six projects locally under the same load (WebKit 14.7 s). With the owner’s approval, pushed `12ecf24`: CI run 44819879540 passed both jobs and Playwright 108 of 108, with the test at 1.1 minutes in WebKit desktop, past the old 60 s limit. Next action: P4B.5a, P4B.6 or P4B.2; look at the `test_practice.py` failure.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.4 built: the TypeScript engine.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible. Resumed the `hu-m1-s0` blueprint run, which had stopped at 252.5 million iterations, then took P4B.4 while a parallel session built P4B.3 in this checkout (it committed `ea1ffa6`, folding in this session's `tsconfig.json` line). Ported `rivermind/engine` to `web/src/lib/engine` (cards, evaluator) and `web/src/lib/server/engine` (state, pots, seating, view, history, `Random` with `cryptoRandom`, fuzz), with a `dumps` that writes what Python's does. Added `rivermind.engine.differential` (fuzzed hands with per-step digests, evaluator digests), `pnpm engine:parity` (also a step in CI's `web` job), `pnpm engine:bench`, and `test.engine.export_fixtures`, which writes the rule hands to `rules.json` (pytest fails while it is stale). The long parity run matched all 800,000 hands and every evaluator digest. Pitfalls: Node runs the engine without a build only while it keeps to erasable TypeScript and relative `.ts` imports (no `$lib`, no enums); `pnpm` passes a literal `--` to scripts, which `parseArgs` rejects, so the scripts drop it; and a SplitMix32 sampler drawing `next() % 52` with redraws is the cheapest generator both languages reproduce exactly. Two kinds of failure under the training load, all passing on rerun: Playwright's chromium-phone “two people and a bot play on through a disconnect” (a passing rerun took 59.4 s of its 60 s) and “waits in line for a full table”; and once in the full pytest, `test/db/test_wallet.py::test_ledgers_balance_after_random_play` failed its chip-accounting check (`test/server/test_wallet.py:294`: people's balances against grants, top-ups and the bots' chips), then passed 5 runs of 5 with the server wallet tests. This session changed no server code, but a chip-accounting miss in the live server's code deserves a look. Checks are in the P4B.4 evidence. Not pushed. Next action: with the owner's approval, push `main` and tick P4B.3 and P4B.4 once CI passes; then P4B.5a, P4B.6 or P4B.2; check the training run.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.3 built: the Drizzle schema and migrations.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; a parallel session held P4B.4 in this checkout, so this one took P4B.3, developed it in a scratch clone of `main` (so `pnpm add` would not disturb the other session’s running tests) and committed only its own files and hunks. `web/src/lib/server/db/` has the schema (from `drizzle-kit pull`, cleaned up), a node-postgres pool of 10, the migrator behind `pnpm db:migrate` and the test-database guard; `web/drizzle/0000_baseline.sql` builds exactly Alembic `0006`. Reworded the first criterion: the tables stay unqualified and reach `app` through `search_path`, as Alembic’s do, instead of `pgSchema('app')`. Pitfalls: `drizzle-kit pull` drops single-column primary-key names (Alembic’s `pk_<table>`) and reverses some composite keys, `drizzle-kit generate` writes `"public".` into foreign keys, and column order must follow the database because later Alembic revisions appended columns; Drizzle records each migration’s SHA-256 and only runs entries newer than the last one recorded. On ubuntu-vm, ran the updated `setup-database.sh` (adds the owner’s empty `drizzle` schema) and adopted a copy of the live database with no change, then dropped the copy. Checks are in the P4B.3 evidence; the web suite (check, lint, Vitest with the database, build, Playwright) and pytest with the database passed in the clone. With the owner’s approval, pushed `ea1ffa6`: CI ran the database tests in both jobs and they passed, so P4B.3 is ticked, but the `web` run was cancelled at its time limit while installing Playwright’s packages. The two runs before it failed one WebKit multiplayer test that passes locally (see **Blockers**). Next action: P4B.2, P4B.5a or P4B.6; find out why the WebKit disconnect test times out in CI.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.1 deployed, with a CSP fix.** The owner approved pushing and deploying `7ba1952`. `update.sh` passed and started `rivermind-poker-web`, but `pnpm e2e:live` failed the Chromium practice reload, and the failure repeated on every run. Cause: Node leaves the policy off a 304, Caddy’s `?Content-Security-Policy` then added its own without the start-up script’s hash, and Chromium applied it to the cached page, which stayed blank. Reproduced it against a local Caddy stack, fixed the Caddy site to set its policy only on its own responses (`2a5dc1c`), and added a 304 check to the live header test. With the owner’s approval, pushed and deployed the fix: 596 server tests passed, 9 tables were restored, and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 with the 2 restart tests skipped. Pitfall: the local Playwright suite does not run through Caddy, so check Caddy changes against a local Caddy (rate limits removed) or the live suite. Next action: P4B.2, P4B.3 or P4B.4.

**2026-11-20 · P4B.1 built: the client on its own Node server.** The owner asked to start P4B.1. `web/` now builds with `adapter-node`, and `web/server/` puts SvelteKit’s handler and a `ws` socket on one HTTP server. It answers `/healthz`, drains on SIGTERM and, with `RIVERMIND_LEGACY_API`, forwards `/api/*` and `/ws` to `serve-nl`. `pnpm build` bundles it as `build/start.js`, so a release needs no `node_modules`, as on the host’s other Node sites. Pages carry SvelteKit’s hashed CSP plus their socket origin, and `scripts/check-inline-code.js` replaces the old bootstrap externalizer. Playwright now runs against the Node server in front of `serve-nl`. Added a criterion: the static deploy cannot serve a Node build, so `update.sh` now publishes a release for the new `rivermind-poker-web.service` (port 4320, dynamic user, system Node 26). Caddy sends pages there and `/api` and `/ws` straight to `serve-nl`, and keeps the pages’ CSP with `?Content-Security-Policy`. P4B.7 now names that unit and port. Pitfalls: adapter-node assumes https unless `ORIGIN` or `PROTOCOL_HEADER` is set; unknown paths are now 404 rather than the SPA fallback (invites use `/?invite=`); the immutable assets’ Cache-Control has no spaces (`public,max-age=31536000,immutable`); and rebuilding `web/build` while Playwright runs breaks the run. Checks are in the P4B.1 evidence; pytest passed with the database tests. Not pushed or deployed. Next action: with the owner’s approval, push and deploy, then run `pnpm e2e:live`; otherwise P4B.2, P4B.3 or P4B.4.

## Phase 4C session handoffs

**2026-11-22 · P4C.9's CI closed out; the six-seat format built in Python and TypeScript and pushed; training held, then allowed again.** The owner asked for the two open items to be closed and the plan carried on, and then, while a first probe was running, said this Mac is under heavy load from two other projects and that training must wait until those are done. **Closed out:** CI runs 45070020678 (`19a160c`) and 45070801572 (`3888572`) passed both jobs; `/healthz` reports `19a160c`; `pnpm e2e:live` passed 21 with 3 skipped. **Built** (`1d58ad5`): the six-seat format `6max-s1`, end to end in Python except a trained blueprint; P5.6's lines and [docs/bots.md](docs/bots.md#6-max-and-9-max) have every figure. **Decisions a later session would wonder about.** (1) Six seats is a graph, not a tree: betting states with the same seats, chips, bets and rights are one node. Without that the smallest size table has 8.5 million decision nodes at six seats; with it `6max-s1` has 0.65 million at 100 big blinds. The bot gives up knowing how a pot was built, only what it is and who is in. (2) One size a level, with all-in always there, and 200 card buckets a street; a second bet size doubles the run. (3) The heads-up walk is untouched and the multiway walk is held to it by a test, rather than replaced by it: the champion's checkpoint is a function of that code. (4) A table of six with two dealt in is not the six-seat game (the button posts the small blind), so the six-seat policy refuses it and P5.7 is to hand those hands to the heads-up champion. (5) The gate at six seats is one candidate against five copies of the opponent, each deal played six times; nothing else is measured. (6) The first `c200` build stands as the table set: its manifest records `3888572` and unmodified card code. (7) A probe of 2.56 million iterations ran for two and a half minutes before the owner's message arrived; nothing has run since, and its run directory is deleted. The pruning threshold still needs a probe on a partly trained run. (8) Nine seats does not fit this Mac at these sizes; the options are in the doc and none is chosen. (9) AGENTS.md gained the commands, four conventions for multiway trees and “ask before heavy local work”. (10) **Then the Node side** (`e906c59`, recorded under P5.7): the format 2 reader, the graphs compiled from the TypeScript engine to Python's SHA-256s, and the multiway policy, with `pnpm bots:parity` playing a synthetic six-seat bundle in both languages. It went ahead of a champion because the bundle format has to be final before one is promoted, and a port is the cheapest way to find out it is not. (11) Measuring why the policy lost its place at chaotic tables found one cause, a raise at a node the graph has closed, and a rule for it (the call), in both languages; what is left is a hand the graph has ended while the real one goes on, and it is recorded, not fixed. **Not done:** any training, the second table build, evaluation, promotion; anything on the site itself; Medium for heads-up. **Checks:** for `1d58ad5`, full pytest 460 at a load average of 110, no web file having changed; for `e906c59`, pytest 460 again, check, lint, Vitest with the database 1,256, the three parity runs, the build and `pnpm e2e --workers 2` (186 passed, 12 skipped) while the load average went from 15 to 200. Nothing was pushed or deployed. Moved the “P4C.4 built” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** Medium for heads-up, or the six-seat run when training may start. The owner then approved the push: `21cc720` is on both remotes. **The owner then lifted the hold**, on the condition that load only slows the work and cannot spoil it, which is so (**Blockers** has why, and what to watch). Waiting on the owner: nothing to decide before the run; a push of this record, and whether Phase 6 may start before Phase 5B closes.

**2026-11-22 · P4C.9: table voice is live; Phase 4C closed, with real phones moved to P7.1b.** The owner approved the launch and whatever it needed, had no phone to offer, named the iOS Simulator for device checks and left every open item to the session's judgment; D-16 records each decision made under that, for the owner to overturn. **Built** (`bcbf4c9`): Caddy's `/voice` route (by path alone, as `/ws`: a handshake over HTTP/2 has no `Upgrade` header to match), `RIVERMIND_VOICE_OFFER=1`, Caddy's `Permissions-Policy`, a cap of 32 TCP connections to 3478 from one address in an nftables table of the site's own that the voice unit loads before it starts, four more sandbox settings for the voice unit, three checks in `smoke.sh`, and `voice.live.ts` in `pnpm e2e:live`. **On the host first** (D-10): the sandbox settings and the cap were tried on a scratch copy of the live unit in its place, with real calls from this Mac, before either went into the repository; the host sees real client addresses on 3478, which a cap by address depends on. **Deployed**: release `20261122T194132Z-bcbf4c9290b2`; every figure is on P4C.9's lines and in [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#the-launch-p4c9). **Checked live**: `pnpm e2e:live` with voice in three engines at two sizes, then by hand with Safari on a simulated iPhone at a table with a Firefox speaker and a Chromium listener, and a minute of each browser's own statistics. **Decisions a later session would wonder about.** (1) Phase 4C is ticked although no real phone has been in voice: the Simulator's Safari is the real engine, and what it cannot stand in for (a phone's processor, microphone and speakers, a mobile network, the path from outside the home network, the delay between two networks, Android) is P7.1b, a step that gates 1.0. I reworded the phase's exit criteria and P4C.9's second criterion to say what was done, and split the unverified parts of P4C.2a and P4C.6 into P7.1b rather than tick or drop them. (2) The Simulator's microphone gives Safari no audio on this Mac, so the simulated phone listened and was heard by nobody; the check page got a call with a tone to tell a silent microphone from a transform that never ran. (3) A second push and deploy the same day (`19a160c`, release `20261122T210849Z-19a160c39c4b`) carried four fixes the session by hand turned up or that were recorded as small: the invitation card, a stale notice, and blockers (2) and (3). I took the owner's “anything else as needed” to cover it and say so here. (4) `races.spec.ts` lost one seed and gained another: which random requests land moves with the order a request awaits things in, and the rejoin fix changed that order (AGENTS.md). (5) The host's own notes (`ubuntu-vm-backup/docs/SERVER.md`) do not yet mention the table `inet rivermind_voice`; that repository is not this session's to edit. (6) One voice room on the live site, minutes after the launch, was somebody else's: an iPhone's Safari on the home address. **Not done:** anything P7.1b names; desktop Safari; a TURN server, which nothing yet shows a need for. **Checks** are on P4C.9's lines; the load on this Mac passed 900 during the first full runs, and every failure there passed alone. Moved the “P4C.3 complete” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P5.6. Waiting on the owner: nothing to decide; P7.1b when there are phones, and a word if any decision in D-16 should go the other way.

The limit of 8 rooms and 72 people stays: the media worker's one core and the unit's `MemoryHigh` decide it, not the uplink, and the doc says what to raise to go further. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) CI run 45052224306 failed at `pnpm voice:check`: P4C.8's keyless-listener check had only ever run on this Mac, and on Linux Chromium's fake microphone sends frames for silence that are a header and a tag over nothing, which the check took for packets that were not SFrame. (2) That fix, the host's figures (with a changed comment in the voice unit, so the next deploy restarts the voice service once more) and this record were pushed after the deploy, under the same request: the live site runs `28fd8d2`, and what follows it on `main` changes no code the site runs. (3) Nothing was measured on the host until the deploy had finished; the load tool ran beside the live services on loopback, niced, for three minutes. (4) A WebKit voice check failed once on this Mac while other sessions' iOS Simulators had its load average at 150 to 670, and passed on the next run (**Blockers**, 8). Checks: the host's own suites in the deploy; `pnpm e2e:live` 18 passed, 2 skipped; for the check's fix, check, lint, the check itself in all three engines here and every voice check in Linux Chromium and Firefox in Docker (59 passed, one Firefox timing check on its retry, 27 skipped). Moved the “P4C.2 complete” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.9, the launch, an owner gate; its code (the Caddy route, the switch, the live voice check) can be written first. Waiting on the owner: the go-ahead for the launch and its session by hand on real phones; P4C.8's three findings (the first now sized: 32 TCP connections to 3478 per address); RNNoise's 20.7 ms; a phone for P4C.2a and P4C.6; a word on blocks and the three-second grace; P6.0c on a real iPhone.

**2026-11-22 · P4C.7 built: rooms that come back by themselves, a limit on voice, and delay and loss measured; delay in Chromium is over its target.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step**. One commit (`9ccaed6`); [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#audio-quality-resilience-and-capacity-p4c7) has every figure and how it was taken. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) A dead media worker is replaced inside the service, which closes every socket with 1012; pages come back by the road a restart already uses. I did not try to keep rooms across it: nothing of a Router outlives its worker. (2) I reworded the recovery criterion: an ICE restart keeps the group, where the plan expected a new one each time. The page starts ICE again when the browser says the network is back (`online`) or a transport loses its path, and falls back to a new session after three seconds. (3) A relay stands in for the network (`voice/check/relay.ts`): it cannot change a browser's own address, so the case where ICE must gather again waits for a real phone (P4C.9). (4) Not in the plan, added because recovery needed them: a room that cannot come back now backs off and ends after two minutes, where it asked for a new token every second for ever; a network that blocks voice's port ends the room with a reason, where the page sat connected and silent; and the table hears when a departed person's grace runs out. (5) P4C.3 had left “a new group when nobody commits a removal” to this step. I did not build it: the members' turns already cover a hung page, and a page that holds because the group has a stranger should go on holding; the doc says why. (6) The limit is 8 rooms and 72 people, cautious on purpose: the line uploads 556 Mbps and more (measured from this Mac, which shares it), and what is unmeasured is ubuntu-vm's own CPU and path. SSH to the host stopped at Tailscale's check all session (**Blockers**, 7), which I did not work around, so nothing was measured or changed there. (7) `pnpm voice:measure` is not in CI: its figures mean nothing on a shared runner. (8) The loss measure plays speech, the Mac's own voice, never kept in the repository: with a tone, concealment equalled loss, because Opus protects only speech; that nearly went down as a failure of the design. (9) Chromium's loopback (P4C.6) costs 53 ms after asking it for 10 ms frames, 73 before; with RNNoise's 30 to 40 ms, Chromium's 95th percentile is 170 to 189 ms against 150, and I left the criterion open rather than loosen it. `rivermind.voice.direct` in `localStorage` plays without the loopback, for the session by hand that can say whether Chrome still cancels echo then. Found on the way: a hello refused after it had opened a room left the room open for good; Firefox drops a path whose reflected address is loopback, so the relay sends from the LAN address; Playwright gives a project's permissions to browsers of other engines launched in a test; Playwright's WebKit as a speaker fills every listener's jitter buffer, which is what P4C.1's 174 ms was. Docker ran the Linux checks and the `tc netem` measure; its image is removed again, and `sch_netem` was loaded in Colima's VM. Checks are on P4C.7's evidence line. Not pushed and not deployed. Moved the “P4C.1 complete” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). Then, with the same tools, the measurement P4C.6 still owed (`7f03f73`): RNNoise against the browser's own suppression through Chromium's capture path on Linux, which also found RNNoise halving the voice's level on the way (its node put out a silent second channel), now fixed; the full web suites passed again after it (Vitest 1,242, Playwright 186 with 12 skipped, `pnpm voice:check` 81 with 6 skipped), once two checks that had each failed once in Firefox were made sturdier. RNNoise alone did 7 dB better than RNNoise after the browser's own suppression, the default; I left the default for a real microphone to confirm. Last, blocker (1) (`8222920`): the practice test's bust now plays a seeded table (`e2e/seeded-server.ts`) and comes in the fourth hand every run, in all six browser projects, where it took 3 to 72 seconds; Playwright 186 with 12 skipped again. **Next:** P4C.9, an owner gate, once the owner has answered. Waiting on the owner: approval to push P4C.8 and P4C.7 (a migration and a changed voice unit ride on the next deploy); which way out for Chromium's delay; a listen to `artifacts/voice-loss/*.wav`; the Tailscale check, so the host can be measured; and the earlier items (P4C.8's three findings, RNNoise's 20.7 ms, a phone for P4C.2a and P4C.6, a word on blocks and the three-second grace, P6.0c on a real iPhone).

**2026-11-22 · P4C.8 built: the keyless listener, voice reports that suspend speaking, limits, and the published hashes of the code.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step**. One commit (`4e1b94b`); [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#privacy-safety-and-moderation-p4c8) has how it works and what was found. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) The keyless listener is a test build, `voice/check/tap-server.ts`, which runs `voice/server.ts` unchanged with mediasoup's observers attached; the service itself gained no way to copy audio. The check judges the copy two ways: by the keys each person held (who left opens nothing after, who arrived nothing before) and by an Opus decoder (the copy holds no tone). (2) A suspension is enforced by the voice service from the seat list's new, optional `silenced`, whose end times end it by themselves, so expiry needs no list; pages hear `silenced` notices and the welcome says it too. (3) The rule counts reports only from people, tables and addresses all different from each other, so friends at one table or guests on one address cannot silence anyone; a further report while three still count carries it on a day from then. Whether the threshold should be told to players (the report form says “three other tables”) is the owner's call. (4) `voice-files.json` lists every file the pages run, not only voice's, because any script on a page can reach its keys; the adapter fixes its static files before the list exists, so the Node server serves it (AGENTS.md). (5) `ReportReason` in protocol v2 gains `voice`, an addition old clients never send; the fixed `table-contract.json` changed by hand in one line, the error text naming the reasons. (6) A socket from this machine with no forwarded address is not limited by address at the voice service, so the checks and a development proxy are not; behind Caddy every socket has one. Found on the way: mediasoup keeps idle TCP connections to the media port open, and a voice socket that never said hello stayed open while it answered pings (now closed after 10 s). Both migration tests and the table contract assumed the baseline was the only migration and now read the journal. The prototype in `web/voice/prototype/` no longer holds anything a later step needs. Checks are on P4C.8's evidence line. Not pushed and not deployed. Moved the “P6.0c: the table fits one phone screen” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.7. Waiting on the owner: approval to push P4C.8 and its record (P4C.6 is already on both remotes at `b02e0d7`, CI run 44974870391 green), with its new migration; the three findings in P4C.8's **Remaining**; and the earlier items (a word on RNNoise's 20.7 ms, a phone for P4C.2a and P4C.6, a word on blocks and the three-second grace, P6.0c on a real iPhone).

**2026-11-22 · P4C.6 built: table voice at the table, RNNoise included; a few checks wait on the owner or a phone.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step**. Two commits (`9f94cec`, `8bd5e7b`); [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#voice-at-the-table-as-a-person-sees-it-p4c6) has how it works and what was found. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) Voice is offered only where `GET /api/voice` says so: a voice service and `RIVERMIND_VOICE_OFFER=1`. The live unit does not set it, so deploying this shows nobody a card that cannot connect; P4C.9 sets it with Caddy's `/voice` route and the `Permissions-Policy` change. Locally and in the browser tests the Node server passes `/voice` on itself (`RIVERMIND_VOICE_PROXY`), never on the live unit. (2) Chromium echo-cancels only audio from a peer connection ([crbug 687574](https://bugs.chromium.org/p/chromium/issues/detail?id=687574)), so the Web Audio mix that volume and panning need would echo on laptop speakers; in Chromium it plays through a local loopback into an audio element. Whether that cancels echo well on real hardware is for P4C.9's session by hand. (3) Voice signalling v1 gained `status` and a `peer` notice rather than a v2: a page sends `status` only when the welcome says `statuses: true`, and pages from before ignore what is new. (4) A voice mute of the listener's own wins over a P4.5 mute, which only starts someone muted; volumes and mutes live in the browser by public reference. (5) Deafen also stops the microphone, and the service pauses every stream for a deafened page, which saves the host's uplink. (6) Push-to-talk shows the person as muted on their own seat only; others see their ring when they talk. Found on the way: Playwright gives every engine its project's user agent string, so a Firefox page claimed to be Chrome (now told apart by `navigator.userAgentData`, in AGENTS.md); a mic stand drawn in ink vanished on the seat's ink tag; the bar overflowed at 375 px once in voice. The screenshot comparison is clean apart from `/style`'s new row. Checks are on P4C.6's evidence line. Then RNNoise (`8bd5e7b`): `@sapphi-red/web-noise-suppressor`'s worklet on a 48 kHz capture context, falling back to the browser's own processing; the page CSP gains `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` alone; notices in `licenses/`. It cuts a room by 12.5 to 30 dB where nobody speaks and keeps speech, at 0.5 to 1% of a core, but adds 20.7 ms against the 20 ms aimed at, by design of the processor's ring and RNNoise's frame; I left that criterion open rather than loosen it, for the owner to accept or not. Found: its worklet starts the WebAssembly asynchronously, so an offline render plays silence unless it waits (AGENTS.md). The Linux run of the new check used Docker; its image is removed again, though Colima's disk image does not shrink (43 GB; **Blockers**, 4). Not pushed and not deployed. Moved the “Host: the voice port is closed” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.8, starting with the keyless listener. Waiting on the owner: approval to push; a word on RNNoise's 20.7 ms; a phone for the hidden-and-shown path, RNNoise's cost and the rest of P4C.2a; a word on blocks and on the three-second grace; the look at P6.0c on a real iPhone.

**2026-11-21 · P4C.5 complete: tokens from the table server, seat lists, blocks and mutes at the voice service, and pages that come back after a restart.** Continuing the same request after P4C.4. [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#voice-at-the-table-p4c5) has the design. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) The table server sends the voice service the whole signed list of who a table has in voice, not changes, and only when it changes; the voice service keeps the latest by its time and refuses anyone it does not name. A token request sends the list first, so a voice service that has just started learns a room before anyone reaches it. (2) I reworded criterion 1: a block no longer removes anyone from the room. The two who blocked each other never hear each other, by the service's consumers, and everyone else still hears both. Taking a blocked person out would let anyone silence anyone for a whole table, and taking them out of the group protects nothing, since the people at a table are not who voice protects against. The owner may want it otherwise. (3) “Bust” is no chips, no rebuy coming and not in the hand; the test plays one to the end rather than writing a stack, because a change with no events reaches no listener (AGENTS.md). (4) Mutes stay in the page, as `VoiceRoom`'s `muted`, and leave the muted person's consumer paused at the service; P4C.6 passes them from `/api/relations`. (5) Found on the way: a departing person's listeners heard `left` before their stream ended, because mediasoup reports the producer closed later; the service now ends those consumers itself first. The web unit gets `RIVERMIND_VOICE_URL`, so the next deploy turns this on with the voice unit. (6) Then the restarts (`69e49ae`): `VoiceRoom` became the page's handle and each connection a `VoiceSession` with its own worker and keys, since a new group starts at epoch 0 and a key store never goes back; it asks for a new token and rejoins after the service restarts, keeping mutes. Playwright's WebKit reports the service's 1012 as 1005 (AGENTS.md). Checks are on the evidence lines. Not pushed and not deployed. **Next:** P4C.6, the voice controls at the table, or P4C.8 beside it. Waiting on the owner: as in the entry below, plus a word on blocks.

**2026-11-21 · P4C.4 built: the voice service, its signalling and its unit; checked on ubuntu-vm, not deployed.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step**. Built in two commits (`f3544a9`, `1f8f6e9`); [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#the-voice-service-as-built-p4c4) has how it works, what it refuses and what was measured. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) Signalling is on 127.0.0.1:4323, not the plan's 4322, which is fieldcadence.app's on the host; the criterion, the design and AGENTS.md say so. (2) Tokens are signed with a key derived from the session secret, `voiceKey` in `tokens.ts`, and the voice unit is given only that (`/var/lib/rivermind-poker/voice-key`, which `update.sh` derives as a dynamic user). The voice service faces the internet through a C++ media worker running as its own user; holding the secret, a compromise of it could forge any guest's session. P4C.5's token endpoint calls `voiceKey` on the secret it already has. (3) A token names the player's public reference, the name the group's leaves carry, never the player id. (4) The voice bundle is built apart from the site's server, and `update.sh` restarts the voice unit only when its unit, its key or `build/voice/` changed, so a site deploy leaves conversations alone, as D-15 wants; I read the criterion's “installs and restarts the unit” that way. Every release's voice service is also checked as a candidate on spare ports before the switch, and `update.sh voice-candidate RELEASE` runs that alone. (5) I built `VoiceRoom` (`web/src/lib/voice/room.ts`), a page's whole side of a room, earlier than planned, because checking the service in real browsers needed it: it commits removals in `commitRank` turns, joins again from the GroupInfo a refusal carries and opens each stream as the leaf the service names. P4C.5 puts it at the table. (6) `Delivery.submit` now takes the leaf the socket holds, so a reloaded page's old leaf cannot commit through the new socket. (7) The announced address is what `rivermindpoker.com` resolves to, looked up each minute; a change restarts the media server and tells pages `media.reset`, and P4C.7 makes pages recover by themselves. (8) The prototype stays for its keyless listener and audio-level control until P4C.8 moves them into a test build. Found on the way: mediasoup in an ES module bundle needs `__dirname`, so the bundle failed on the host and ran here only because `web/node_modules` sat above it (now in AGENTS.md); and a transport's `id` overwrote the request's in its answer. On ubuntu-vm (D-10): a scratch build under `/home/sawyer/web-work`, a scratch release in `/srv/rivermind-poker/voice-scratch` run by a transient copy of the unit with a key from a test secret, `ufw` allowing 3478 for the run, and `update.sh voice-candidate` on ports 4400 and 4401; all removed afterwards, with `ufw` closed again, and `/healthz` still reports `7116e1d`. What stays is `/var/lib/rivermind-poker/voice-key` (root-only), the key the first deploy would write. `web/scripts/voice-check-linux.sh` now lets mediasoup fetch its worker, since the room checks start the service. Checks are on the evidence line. Not pushed and not deployed. **Next:** P4C.5. Waiting on the owner: approval to push (this and the record of the last push) and to deploy, which installs the voice unit, opens 3478 and starts it; a phone off the home network for the outside path; and the earlier items (the rest of P4C.2a, a word on the three-second grace, the look at P6.0c on a real iPhone).

**2026-11-21 · P4C.3 complete: the group key from MLS, the voice code and the seat check.** Continuing the same request after P4C.2. Built in three commits: the working group’s test vectors against ts-mls, the group with the service’s delivery rules, and the epoch keys with the voice code. [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#the-group-key-as-built-p4c3) has how each works and the measurements. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) The MLS test vectors are fetched, not committed: `mlswg/mls-implementations` states no license. `load.ts` fetches each file at a pinned commit and checks its hash; CI fails without them and anything else reports the checks skipped, so the Vitest step in CI and on the host now reaches GitHub once a run (the host is not CI, so a deploy does not wait on it). The runners are ts-mls’s own tests, adapted (MIT, `licenses/ts-mls.txt`). (2) The service checks commits by the public trees before and after, because it cannot check a membership tag: a newcomer adds one leaf, their own, and a member removes only what the service asked for. Members enforce the same on their side through ts-mls’s callback. No resync is used anywhere. (3) `Delivery.submit` returns the removals it proposes again after each commit, and they go to everyone including the newcomer. A test found why: a proposal dies with its epoch, and a member who missed one cannot apply the commit that follows. P4C.4 must send them so. (4) The sender moves to a new epoch at once and listeners wait up to 250 ms for a key they do not hold yet (`decryptor` in `sframe-transform.ts`), where the plan had only a grace window: a delayed sender would have sealed under a key the leaver holds. (5) The voice code is four BIP-39 words (MIT, `licenses/python-mnemonic.txt`), stretched in sixty chained PBKDF2 rounds. One long call stalled Firefox’s WebCrypto queue and every listener’s audio with it; that pitfall is in AGENTS.md. Its strength, and why 44 bits needs the stretching, is in the doc. (6) I reworded criterion 4 in two ways and said so on it: the warning itself is P4C.6’s to show (added to its criteria), and someone who has just left a seat has three seconds before they count as a stranger. Without that every speaker went silent for the length of a removal each time anyone stood up, which criterion 3 forbids; someone never seated still gets none. This is a judgment about a security rule, so the owner may want it tighter. (7) `E` = 4 and `S` = 6 stay. Not built here, and named in the doc: the wire (P4C.4), the client’s timing of commits and retries (P4C.5), and recovery when nobody commits a removal (P4C.7). The Linux run is now a script, `web/scripts/voice-check-linux.sh` (`e221033`), for the next change to these checks; its 3.6 GB image is removed again from this Mac. Checks are on the evidence line. Not pushed and not deployed: nothing the site serves changed. Moved the “P5.5, P5.7a and the start of P6.1 pushed and deployed” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.4. The owner then approved the push: both remotes are at `9319e86`, and CI run 44960250047 passed both jobs with `pnpm voice:check` and the MLS vectors in it for the first time, which ticks P4C.2a’s first criterion. Nothing was deployed; this note is local. Waiting on the owner: the iOS Simulator or a phone for the rest of P4C.2a, a word on the three-second grace, and the look at P6.0c on a real iPhone.

**2026-11-21 · P4C.2 complete: SFrame to the RFC’s vectors, and the worker that seals and opens every frame.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; `main` was current with both remotes, so took **Next step**. What was built and found is in [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#frame-encryption-as-built-p4c2) and the evidence line. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) I split off **P4C.2a** and reworded two criteria. The cost “on a phone” cannot be measured from here: a phone needs a secure context, so it cannot load a test page from this Mac. And CI’s own run of the new step needs a push. Both moved to P4C.2a with a third, Safari itself, so P4C.3 is not held up by things only the owner can unblock; nothing was dropped. (2) The page gives the worker one non-extractable HKDF key an epoch, and the worker derives each member’s AES key inside WebCrypto, where the plan had the page post each key. A page then holds one key however many are seated, and no AES key ever exists as bytes. (3) A receiver’s transform is told which leaf its stream belongs to and refuses anyone else’s frames (`wrong-sender`): without it the server could route one person’s voice into another’s seat. P4C.3 and P4C.5 must pass that leaf to `FrameCrypto.decrypt`. (4) `E` = 4 and `S` = 6, the RFC’s example, stand in until P4C.3 fixes them (`LAYOUT` in `sframe.ts`). (5) What P4C.3 must respect in `KeyStore`: epochs are added once and in order; `sendAs` goes forward only and never back to an epoch already sent under; `retire` ends an epoch, and a page that retires the epoch it sends under sends nothing until it is given another. Give listeners a new epoch before the speaker moves to it. (6) The prototype now seals with the site’s worker, and its own `frame.ts` and `transform.worker.ts` are deleted; the engines’ launch settings moved to `web/voice/check/browsers.ts`. (7) `web/src/lib/voice/sframe-vectors.json` is copied from the RFC, so `licenses/RFC9605.txt` carries the IETF Trust’s notice. Found on the way: Firefox hands a receiver’s transform its peer’s padding as frames with no bytes (counted `empty`, apart from `malformed`); Playwright’s WebKit for Linux has no `RTCRtpScriptTransform`; with no sound device Firefox’s `AudioContext.resume()` never settles; and Chromium’s fake microphone, dead on this Mac, works on Linux. A read of my own key store before committing found that retiring the newest epoch let a page start an older one again from counter 0, a repeated nonce; `sendAs` now refuses any epoch at or below the last one sent under, with a test. Tried and left: the iOS Simulator, to run the check page in Safari itself. This session’s request for access to it went unanswered, so it was not worked around; the page keeps a run-by-hand mode for it (P4C.2a). The Linux run used Docker on this Mac with Playwright’s image, which is removed again. Meanwhile another session recorded the push of P4C.1 (`c08be98`); CI run 44950544786 on it has since passed both jobs. Checks are on the evidence line. Not pushed and not deployed: nothing the site serves changed. Moved the “P6.1 started” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.3, or P4C.4 beside it. Waiting on the owner: approval to push (which gives `pnpm voice:check` its first CI run), access to the iOS Simulator or a phone for P4C.2a, and the look at P6.0c on a real iPhone.

**2026-11-21 · P4C.1 complete: the voice prototype checked the design, and mediasoup and ts-mls both hold.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; `main` was already current (one docs commit ahead of the remotes), so took **Next step**. Built the prototype in `web/voice/prototype/` (a mediasoup service, a page on mediasoup-client, an encrypting worker and its Playwright checks, `pnpm voice:proto`) and the group-key check `web/src/lib/voice/mls-design.spec.ts`. What they showed is in [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md#what-the-prototype-found-p4c1), which is corrected where the prototype disagreed: bandwidth (about 103 kbps for each listener and speaker and 7.4 Mbps for a full table, not 90 and 6.5), who publishes the GroupInfo (whoever commits; the service cannot make one), how a reloaded page rejoins (as a newcomer, and the service removes its old leaf), how ts-mls is imported (by subpath), and what a release carries for mediasoup (its worker binary). Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) I reworded P4C.1’s first criterion. Chromium’s fake microphone does not work on this Mac: `getUserMedia` never settles with it, in Playwright’s Chromium and in Chrome 157, whatever the launch settings. Chromium was fed a Web Audio tone instead, which takes the same road from the Opus encoder on, and a new criterion in P4C.2 runs the worker’s checks on CI’s Linux, where Chromium’s fake device and Playwright’s other WebKit build get their turn. (2) Playwright’s WebKit could be tested only with a browser per person, the microphone granted and no Web Audio: it gathers ICE candidates only on a page that has captured, and an `AudioContext` stops about half a second after capture starts. So P4C.6 cannot check volume, the speaking ring or spatial audio in Playwright’s WebKit, nor a person who only listens; Chromium, Firefox and a real Safari cover those. Whether Safari shares any of it is unknown until P4C.9. (3) The three packages are dev dependencies like every other, pinned exactly, with `mediasoup` in `allowBuilds`: every install now downloads its worker binary from GitHub (**Blockers**, 5). (4) The prototype is not in CI or `pnpm e2e`: it needs the LAN address (Firefox’s ICE fails against loopback alone) and it is replaced by P4C.2 to P4C.4. Its ts-mls spec is an ordinary Vitest file and does run in CI. (5) LiveKit is not needed. (6) Found in ts-mls 1.6.4: its package root imports a package it does not declare, and `joinGroupExternal` with `resync` set never returns when no leaf carries the joiner’s signature key; both are in AGENTS.md with the other pitfalls. Left open, on docs/voice.md’s list: WebKit’s jitter buffer held 174 ms in the nine-person room against about 35 ms elsewhere, which P4C.7’s 150 ms target has to look at first; the prototype’s frame is SFrame in shape only. On ubuntu-vm (D-10): a scratch install under `/home/sawyer/web-work` and a self-test in `/srv/rivermind-poker/voice-selftest`, run by a transient unit that bound 3478 for under a second with `ufw` still closed; both are removed, the live service was not touched and `/healthz` still reports `7116e1d`. Checks are on the evidence line, at load 4 to 7. Not deployed: nothing the site serves changed. The owner then approved the push: both remotes are at `532b990`. Moved the P5.7a handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** P4C.2 (SFrame against RFC 9605’s vectors), or P4C.4 beside it. Waiting on the owner: the look at P6.0c on a real iPhone.

**2026-11-20 · D-15: table voice, planned as Phase 4C.** The owner asked for live, end-to-end encrypted group voice at the table as the site’s headline feature, built in TypeScript and working before 1.0, with easy self-mute, per-player mute and mute all. Researched the options and wrote [docs/voice.md](docs/voice.md): a mediasoup forwarding server (3.32, Node 22 or later) in a second Node service, `rivermind-poker-voice`, with SFrame (RFC 9605) frame encryption in an `RTCRtpScriptTransform` worker, which Safari 15.4, Firefox 117 and Chrome 141 all support, keyed from an MLS group (RFC 9420, ts-mls 1.6, unaudited, so its interop vectors run in CI). Discord’s DAVE protocol uses the same design. A mesh was rejected because it hands every player everyone’s IP address, and LiveKit is the documented fallback. Added D-15 (it amends D-13’s order and the Q-4 default, and adds a service beside D-2’s), Q-9 (the router port and uplink), Q-10 (who hears voice) and Q-11 (WebAssembly noise suppression), Phase 4C with steps P4C.1 to P4C.9, and voice criteria in P5.6’s entry, P6.5, P6.6, P6.7, P6.7a, P7.1, P7.1a, P7.3, P7.5 and the release checklist. Checked on ubuntu-vm: it sits behind the home router at 192.168.50.2, `ufw` allows only 80, 443 and RustDesk’s ports, and 127.0.0.1:4330 is taken by another site, so the voice signalling port is 4322 and media uses 3478. The owner then answered: the router forwards 3478 and the owner is proxying it on the Windows host (Q-9), voice is for seated people with an 18+ note (Q-10), and voice gets RNNoise noise suppression with `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` added to the CSP for it (Q-11, a new P4C.6 criterion, `@sapphi-red/web-noise-suppressor`, chosen because it runs as an AudioWorklet in all three engines). Opened 3478 UDP and TCP in `ufw` on ubuntu-vm (D-10); nothing listens on it yet. Docs only, so nothing to deploy. Next: P4C.1 (the voice prototype) in parallel with P4B.2 or P4B.5b.

**2026-11-21 · P5.7a complete: the champion is playable as RiverMind Hard, built and not deployed.** Continuing the same request after P5.5. (1) The bot kind is `hard` and its label **RiverMind Hard**, the top of the Easy, Medium, Hard ladder P5.7 plans; for now it plays two seats only, and `Site.botPlays` is where a format's table size is enforced. (2) The Node server checks a bundle against `web/src/lib/champions.json`, an extract of the registry that `blueprint promote` writes and pytest keeps current, rather than reading `docs/` at run time: a release then carries the registry it was built with. `/api/config` describes the champion (name and the gate's results for the served strategy), so the lobby shows what the server loaded, and `/bots` shows the full evaluations. (3) One policy a table (`forTable`): `BlueprintPolicy` remembers 256 hands, and a single shared memory could drop a slow table's hand on a busy site. (4) A saved table whose bot a server does not run now plays on against RiverMind Easy instead of being closed at start-up, in both servers. Closing it would have lost the chips of a wallet-backed table after a rollback to `serve-nl`, which runs no trained bot. (5) Switching opponents happens inside the runner's change once the bot is out of the hand, with no new table and no hand dealt in between; a switch asked mid-hand is not saved, so a restart during that hand forgets it and the guest asks again. (6) `serve-nl` got the same practice request and the fallback, and nothing else: its recorded contracts are byte for byte as before, because the two routes with new optional fields now leave `null`s out. (7) `update.sh` refuses to deploy while a registered champion's files are missing from `/srv/rivermind-poker/models/champions`, so promote, copy, then deploy. Added to the plan: nothing; the step's criteria stand as written. Pitfalls: the browser tests' practice stack is three big blinds, so a bot that bets at random busts the guest in a hand or two and a test must click “Play again” as it plays (`playToHand`); phones hide avatars, so assert they exist, not that they are visible; `playwright test --project a file` reads the file as a second project, so put the file first; a content page's table needs a narrow layout of its own (the `/bots` table drops a column and wraps the interval below 460 px). **2026-11-21 · P5.5 complete: the promotion gate, the model registry and the first two champions.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step**. `rivermind-poker blueprint promote REPORT --served BUNDLE_REPORT` puts a run to the gate and records the decision in `docs/models.json`, promoted, failed or inconclusive; the champions and decisions part of `docs/models.md` is generated from it, and pytest replays every published decision from its reports. `blueprint evaluate` takes a serving bundle as a candidate or an opponent, and `blueprint champions --verify` checks kept files against the registry. Changed the plan in two ways, both additions. (1) Reworded P5.5's first criterion so the gate must also hold for the serving bundle, as **Now** asked: the bundle's `uint8` strategy on its own card-table arrays is what people will play, so that is what a strength claim should rest on. (2) A promoted run's checkpoint and bundle are hard-linked into `artifacts/models/<name>/`, because the background run keeps ten checkpoints and its last one would have deleted `000128000000`, the checkpoint behind the published P5.4 report. Ran it for real: `hu-m1-s0-128m` was promoted first (the served rounding moved its results by 0.4 and 1.2 bb/100), then `hu-m1-s0-1024m` was evaluated on the same plan plus the champion and replaced it, +21.38 bb/100 (+10.71 to +32.05) head to head. It wins less from the calling station (+226.66 against +308.33) and the min-bet probe than the earlier checkpoint did, and about the same from RiverMind Easy; the note is on the registry page. Copied both to ubuntu-vm under `/srv/rivermind-poker/models/champions/` (D-10; root-owned, world-readable, 2.3 GB with the tables, 37 GB free) and compared every registered file's SHA-256 with the registry. The host's nightly `ubuntu-vm-backup` is a Git repository of configuration and databases and does not suit files of this size, so the documented backup is the two machines plus the fact that tables and runs rebuild byte for byte from their seeds. Pitfalls: macOS's `rsync` (openrsync) has no `--chmod=D755,F644`, so set modes on the host; `blueprint evaluate` marks a report “with local changes” when `rivermind/` is dirty, so commit before evaluating; compiling both trees takes about 2 s under load, and a module of tests that reads many checkpoints and bundles should compile them once (a module fixture in `test_registry.py`). Checks, at load 60 to 120 with the training run and other sessions: pytest with the database 626 of 626, twice (before each commit); the official served report of `hu-m1-s0-128m` matched a dry run from before the commit apart from the commit, the dirty flag and the output directory; no web files changed, so the web suite was not run. Not pushed and nothing to deploy. Moved the P4.2/P4.4 handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). Decisions for the owner: none blocking; this Mac's disk is at 98%. Next action: P5.7a; and when the run reaches 1.28 billion iterations, evaluate its last checkpoint against `hu-m1-s0-1024m` and put it to the gate.

**2026-11-20 · P5.4 complete: blueprint play and evaluation.** Continuing the same request. Added `BlueprintPolicy` (the mapping, re-entry and nearest-bucket fallback from [docs/bots.md](docs/bots.md#mapping-real-actions-onto-the-tree)), the min-bet probe, duplicate seat-rotated matches with `rivermind-poker blueprint evaluate` and `gate` (`0c31c60`, `565daef`, `f362a5e`), and evaluated the run's 128-million checkpoint (`6de6ee0`): +61.1 bb/100 (+50.9 to +71.4) against RiverMind Easy and +308.3 against the calling station, so it passes the P5.5 gate. Copied the hu-m1 card tables to ubuntu-vm (`/srv/rivermind-poker/models/tables/hu-m1`, verified) under D-10. Pitfalls: the first draws of `Random(0)` to `Random(1999)` fall below 0.583 62% of the time, so a sampling test needs one generator stream, not a seed per trial. A policy's per-hand memory must not outlive a block of deals, or a match would depend on how deals were shared among workers, so blocks get fresh policies and the harness calls `new_hand()`. Checks are in the P5.4 evidence. The owner then asked to finish without starting P5.5, and to publish and deploy: pushed `bed85b3` to both remotes and ran `deploy/update.sh` (596 server tests passed, 2 skipped without the card tables; no migrations; 3 open tables restored; release `20261120T151711Z-bed85b3c277e`), and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 of 14 with the 2 restart tests skipped. **2026-11-20 · P5.3 complete: the heads-up trainer.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible. Added `rivermind/bots/blueprint` and `rivermind-poker blueprint` (`b6fa49f`, `d2dd215`): trees compiled from the engine, batched MCCFR with linear discounting, pruning and a regret floor, deterministic across worker counts, with exact resume and atomic, hashed checkpoints. Then started the first blueprint run, `artifacts/blueprints/hu-m1-s0`, at low priority. Pitfalls: every chunk of a batch starts from the same regrets, so the number of batches limits progress (on Kuhn poker 25 batches of 64,000 left an exploitability of 0.078, against 0.011 for 750 of 400), and tests train Kuhn in small batches. Trained play visits nearly twice the nodes of uniform play, so a run takes about twice the P5.1 estimate. Pluribus's pruning threshold is too deep for a run this size. Summed RSS double-counts memory-mapped tables. Other sessions loaded this Mac heavily during the measurements (load averages from 100 to over 600), which slowed training by half or more at times. Checks are in the P5.3 evidence. Not pushed. Next action: P5.4, the evaluation harness, tested on the run's checkpoints; check `artifacts/blueprints/hu-m1-s0/train.log` and resume the run if it stopped.

Pitfalls: scikit-learn's k-means is not bitwise repeatable (its parallel centre sums vary), so the build has its own; building the river board index in each worker would have needed about 2 GB per process, so workers memory-map the saved index; and the build took a tenth of the time P5.1 estimated, because the turn and flop histograms read the river rows instead of evaluating hands again.
