## Phase 5B session handoffs

**2026-11-22 · P5.6's run started and its first checkpoint promoted as the first six-seat champion; RiverMind Medium built, evaluated and recorded (P5.7).** The owner lifted the hold on training and asked for the "Next step" list in order, without stopping except at an owner gate. **Done, in that order.** (1) `c200` built a second time (six workers, from `ca7965d`): all 15 data files byte-identical to the first build. (2) The pruning probe: 24 million iterations with the run's schedule, then regrets scaled to their size later in the run; **the threshold is −60,000,000 and the floor −66,000,000**, which cut visits by as much at mid-run (32%) as `hu-m1`'s choice did (31%), prune nothing at 80 million and 43% by the end. (3) The run `artifacts/blueprints/6max-s1-s0` started at 18:05 on eight workers (**Background run** has its command and how to resume it); it holds 19,000 to 20,000 iterations a second beside other work, about 52 hours to 3.6 billion. (4) RiverMind Medium for heads-up (`5a93b0c`), evaluated and recorded; every figure is on P5.7's lines. **Decisions a later session would wonder about.** (1) The probe found six-seat regrets far larger than docs/bots.md had guessed (it feared −10,000,000 would prune nothing; it would have pruned 60% of visits at mid-run), so the threshold went the other way, six times `hu-m1`'s. (2) So that the run records a clean commit while Medium was still uncommitted, I set aside the four modified files under `rivermind/` with `git stash` while the trainer recorded its identity, and restored them once its first batch was logged; none of them is training code, and the run's checkpoints will say `ca7965d`, clean, which the first one (at 100 million) should be checked for. (3) Medium mixes a hand at a time, not a decision at a time: every line it plays is one of the two bots' own, and its expected result against a fixed opponent is the mean of theirs, which the evaluation bore out against all five opponents measured. The share is one half. (4) Medium is not put to a gate; `blueprint level` records it under the champion it was made from, so a new champion has no Medium until its own is evaluated, and the server offers Medium only for a champion with a recorded one (D-7). (5) The lobby used to show one trained bot; it now shows each, Medium first. (6) Eight workers rather than ten, since this Mac had 43 GB of swap in use from other projects when the run started; memory free stayed at 28 to 37%. (5) **Then the first six-seat checkpoint**, at 100 million iterations: a 4,000-deal look in a scratch directory already beat a table of five RiverMind Easy, so it went to the gate in full and passed as checkpoint and as served bundle: `6max-s1-s0-100m` is the first six-seat champion (+21.14, +15.46 to +26.83, against five RiverMind Easy as served). I promoted it rather than wait for a stronger checkpoint because the gate is the rule and later checkpoints replace it through the same gate; it also gave the six-seat pipeline its first real run end to end, the Node reader included (the long parity run on its bundle is identical). Promoting it put a champion in the site's extract that the server does not serve, so `/bots` now marks such a champion “Not offered at the tables yet” and describes six-way duplicate matches as such, and a Vitest check that expected no six-seat champion now expects this one. **Not done:** serving the six-seat champion (P5.7, **Next step**); a six-seat Medium; nine seats, whose design can now be chosen; a deploy of Medium. **Checks** for `5a93b0c` are on P5.7's lines. For `6d93580` (Medium's evaluation, the registry, `champions.json`): full pytest 468 and Vitest with the database 1,265 again; the server and pages checked by hand as P5.7 says. For the six-seat champion's commit: full pytest 468, check, lint, Vitest with the database 1,265, the build, and `pnpm e2e --workers 4` with the database 192 passed and 12 skipped at a load average of 120 to 220, after a first full run caught the `/bots` browser test assuming a single champion (fixed: it now reads each champion's own section, and checks Medium's and the unserved six-seat one's); `/bots` looked at in the browser pane against the built server. Moved the “P4C.5 complete” handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). **Next:** the six-seat checkpoints, as **Next step** says. **The owner then approved a commit, a push and a deploy:** `a0905a4` is on both remotes and live (release `20261123T005321Z-a0905a4c6fd3`), with RiverMind Medium offered on the site; the six-seat champion's files were copied to ubuntu-vm first because the deploy checks every registered champion, though nothing serves it yet. The figures are on P5.7's **Deployed** line. Waiting on the owner: whether Phase 6 may start before Phase 5B closes.

**2026-11-21 · P6.0c: the table fits one phone screen; pushed and deployed.** The owner sent five screenshots from an iPhone 17 Pro in Brave: on entering a table the action panel covered their own cards and seat, and seeing them meant scrolling the top seats away. Three causes: the header, the bar and the seat tools stood above a table at least 420px tall on a 677px screen, the dock is pinned to the bottom over whatever is there, and the table opened wherever the lobby had been scrolled to. Now the first screen is the game on a phone and an upright tablet (`2b68c2d`): what stands above the table, the table and a zone as tall as the action panel add up to `100svh`; a phone leaves the site header out; the seat tools moved under the table into that zone; the log is below. Fitting nine pods into a felt 377px tall meant a new tall seat map (one row above the board, the eighth and ninth on the rail's bottom corners) and bets placed by pixels under or above their pods; bots' pods show “Easy 4” instead of “RiverMin…”. I added the steps P6.0c and P6.0d because no step covered this, and changed two conventions in AGENTS.md and docs/design.md: the seat tools are now under the table and covered by the turn's panel, where they were above the table so that nothing covered them. **2026-11-21 · P6.1, P4B.8a and P5.9's local check complete: replay from the table, built and not deployed; Phase 4B closed.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible; took **Next step** and finished P6.1 from its **Remaining** line. What was built is in the P6.1 evidence. Decisions a later session would wonder about. (1) “Review last hand” is in the hand log's heading, not the seat tools the plan named: with the practice table's “Opponent” button the tools already fill a phone's width, and a second row pushed the table down until the action panel covered the player's own seat. (2) On an upright screen the replay opens above the table, not in the hand log's place below the dock: the dock grows when the turn comes, and anything under it would move under the player's finger towards Fold. Opening scrolls only as far as the replay's heading, so on a wide screen nothing scrolls and the action panel stays in view. (3) The model reveals all-in hands when the betting ends (rule 12.1), which the criterion's “at the recorded street” asks for; `HandDetail` does not say when hands were tabled, so `buildReplay` derives it as the engine does (at most one player still in with chips behind, and board cards to come). (4) The dock's “Review hand” shows only to a player dealt into the hand, and a miss says so. Found on the way: `/hands` on the live site keeps the first opened hand's board when another hand is opened over it, because Svelte 5.58 does not update the items of an each block whose list is built inline in the template (now in AGENTS.md, with a browser test); and `moderation` (reports, signals, rename) went with `serve-nl` and has no replacement, so added **P4B.8a** for it rather than tick Phase 4B with a tool missing, and then built it (`a5a9aff`): `pnpm moderation`, bundled into a release as `build/moderation.js`. P4B.8's docs criterion is ticked: CONTRIBUTING's table-server section is rewritten around the TypeScript server. A mistake worth knowing: my second “before” screenshot run started after editing had begun and `pnpm visual` builds the working tree, so it captured the half-made change and the first comparison passed pixels it should have flagged; the baselines in the evidence are from a `git archive` copy of `e2f4b8b` (AGENTS.md says how). Then P5.9's first criterion (`6b4358e`): the journey runs against one built Node server on the disposable test database, shared by every browser project, because two servers on one database would each restore and run the other's tables; it is skipped locally without `RIVERMIND_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, and `pnpm e2e` with it must not run beside `pnpm test`. `journey.live.ts` is the same journey for the live check, and passed against a local server with the registered champion at live timing. Last full runs, at load 35 to 45 with training in the background: pytest 438; check, lint, Vitest with the database 676, the three parity checks and the build; `pnpm e2e --workers 4` with the database 150 of 150. State at close: `main` is five commits ahead of both remotes, nothing pushed or deployed, the live site untouched; the training run is at 1,253 of 1,280 million iterations. Moved the D-15 planning handoff to [docs/build-history.md](docs/build-history.md). Next action: with the owner's approval, push, deploy and run `pnpm e2e:live` to finish P5.9; otherwise P4C.1.

Moved and renamed it to `web/art/cards/` (`fronts/<rank>-<suit>.png`, `backs/<name>.png`; the original names are listed in [docs/design.md](docs/design.md#the-cards)), deleted the P6.0 faces, four-colour set, backs and `cards.pxl`, and made `pnpm art` shrink the sprites to 71 × 95 and remove stray card files. The deck’s index is half the old cards’ relative size, so cards now snap to whole device pixels per art pixel and the cards a phone pod or the hand list shows sit in a compact fanned window at 2 or more device pixels per art pixel; the red classic back is the default, and the pods’ badge was redrawn to match. Added Q-8 (the sprites’ source and license) and reworded P6.5’s settings item, since the deck is always four-colour now. Pitfalls: zsh arrays are 1-based, which shifted a first rename by one rank (caught by a contact sheet); the P4.6 `/hands` page overflowed a phone with the larger cards until it used `.card-peek`. Checks are in the P6.0a evidence. Committed `b4492df`; not pushed or deployed. Took that project’s approach: art as text grids with a locked, named palette, a deterministic generator with a drift check, contact-sheet review, whole-number scaling and a hand-drawn pixel font (its TrueType and WOFF writers were ported to `web/scripts/pixel-art/`). ## Phase 4 session handoffs

**2026-11-20 · P4.2/P4.4: a paid sit could land on a closed table.** Asked to find why `test/db/test_wallet.py::test_ledgers_balance_after_random_play` failed once in a full run at load 55 to 70. It is a real chip-accounting bug in `serve-nl`, now fixed. Reproduced it with a scratch harness that ran `random_play` 72 times in memory and 6 times with PostgreSQL (seed 7) beside ten busy loops and the blueprint run (load 80 to 180), and logged every change a runner applied after its table closed. Memory seed 406 came up 4,000 chips short (`paid_in`, `bots_injected` and `bots_held` were right; the people's balances were short): a `take_seat` was waiting on the lock or a worker thread while the table's last person left. The table closed and paid out its seats, and then the hold and the paid sit were applied to the stopped runner. Nothing saves, publishes or pays out a closed table, so the buy-in was lost. A plain sit, and a rebuy into a table evicted for idleness, could do the same. Fix: `TableRunner.sit` and `rebuy` refuse once the runner is closed (a new `unknown_table` table error, the protocol's code for a closed table), and their callers' existing refunds return the chips. `test_chips_paid_while_the_table_closes_come_back` (sit, take_seat, rebuy) holds the payment in its thread while the table closes; it failed all three cases before the fix and passes after. Also fixed a test-only race: `random_play` counted top-ups as the balance after a visit minus the balance before, which also counted a cash-out a table paid in between, so it now sums the ledger's grant and top-up entries. After both fixes, 96 of 96 memory runs and 4 of 4 PostgreSQL runs balanced under the same load; one of them (seed 614) refused a late sit and refunded it. Not fixed: a table closed mid-hand pays back stacks but not the pot. Only idle eviction can do that, and a live hand publishes a timeout at every turn deadline, so it never sits idle for 24 hours. Added the closed-table rule to AGENTS.md for the TypeScript table server. Checks: the full Python suite with `RIVERMIND_TEST_DATABASE_URL` (609 passed, load 165 to 240), and `test/server` alone (169 passed). No web files changed. Not pushed or deployed. Next action: with the owner's approval, push `main` and deploy, since the fix is to the live server.

**2026-11-19 · Fixed the durability flake from the deploy.** The owner asked to fix the test that failed once on ubuntu-vm. Cause: Starlette’s test client cancels the server’s socket handler as soon as a test’s socket closes; when that landed during the save of the disconnect, the save could be dropped before its worker thread started (the store one change behind), or, if the save had committed, its bookkeeping and the publish were skipped, so the next save could pay departing chips again. The same could happen in production whenever a request’s task is cancelled. Every request to a `TableRunner` now goes through `_change`, which applies, saves, accounts for and publishes the change in its own task shielded from the caller. New test: `test_a_change_is_saved_and_published_even_if_its_caller_is_cancelled` (failed before the fix, passes after). The durability test passed 10 of 10 under eight busy CPU loops; pytest 549 passed with the database tests and `pnpm e2e` 90 of 90. With the owner’s approval, pushed and deployed `e99ca90`: `deploy/update.sh` passed all 549 server tests on its first run and restored the open table, and `pnpm e2e:live` passed 14 of 14 with the restart test. Next action: the owner to name the card sprites’ source for Q-8; then P5.1.

**2026-11-19 · P4.5, P4.6 and P6.0a deployed.** The owner asked to push and redeploy. Pushed `main` (`7be243a`) to both remotes; P4.5 and P4.6 were already pushed. The first `deploy/update.sh` stopped before changing the live site: the durability test `test_tables_are_saved_before_publishing_and_restored_at_startup` failed once (connected false, saved seq one behind live, no save failures, 15 s) while 547 passed; its file passed six reruns on ubuntu-vm, and the second run passed 548, migrated to `0005`, installed the Caddy `safety` zone and restored one table. `pnpm e2e:live` passed 12 of 12 plus 2 of 2 for the restart test. Next action: find why the save after a disconnect can lag under full-suite load on the server (the test, or the store’s worker-thread save); the owner to name the card sprites’ source for Q-8; then P5.1.

**2026-11-19 · P4.5 and P4.6 complete; Phase 4 complete.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible and answered Q-1: no logins or user accounts until after 1.0 (D-12, P4.7 declined, sign-in listed in Phase 8). P4.5 (`5c273cc`): app rate limits, preset reactions, reports, mutes and blocks by seat, quick seat keeping blocked players apart, a same-address collusion signal, a handle filter and `rivermind-poker moderation`. P4.6 (`498be82`): hand seats stored per hand, `GET /api/hands`, the `/hands` and `/fairness` pages. Pitfalls: `create_app` now applies rate limits by default, so tests that hammer the app pass `limits=safety.UNLIMITED` (the `Server` used directly has none); a hidden browser pane pauses CSS animations, so an entrance animation sits on its transparent first frame and looks broken (finish them with `document.getAnimations()` before a screenshot); a parallel session redrawing the card art had staged deletions and half-finished edits in the shared tree, which broke the client build there, so the final checks ran in a scratch copy and P4.6 was committed through a temporary index with only its own files. Checks are in the step evidence. Not pushed or deployed. Next action: with the owner's approval, push and deploy P4.5 and P4.6 (migrations `0004` and `0005`, the new Caddy zone) and run `pnpm e2e:live`; otherwise start P5.1, the bot abstraction design in `docs/bots.md`.

Pitfalls: under load, random-play coverage checks failed while the ledgers held, so timing-dependent kinds now only need an attempt; forced clicks on the hidden lobby radios raced the table list's refresh on phones, and Playwright's `dispatchEvent` crashes Firefox pages, so the helper calls the input's own `click()`. **2026-11-19 · P4.3 complete.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible. Built the lobby: a live table list with filters, quick seat, seats taken from bots (at once between hands, otherwise held until the bot's hand ends), 60-second held seats, a waitlist, private tables with invite links, and spectating with “Sit here” on empty seats. Holds and the waitlist are table-core state (rule 2.4 in [docs/rules.md](rules.md)), saved in snapshot format 2; bots refill a seat nobody wants. Pitfalls: inserting the lobby actions into the wallet's random play halved the rebuy band and made its coverage check flaky; advancing the `ManualClock` there made turn timeouts depend on thread timing, so hold expiry is tested deterministically instead. The new lobby has its own “Sit down” buttons and “Blinds” filter, so browser tests reach the new-table form through `newTableForm()`. The hand log now names each event's player as of that moment. Checks are in the P4.3 evidence. Committed `dedc77c`; not pushed or deployed (the deploy also installs the new Caddy rate limit). Next action: P4.4, multi-human correctness (one seat per player is already enforced; add concurrent-action race tests and a two-context browser test with disconnects).

**2026-11-19 · P4.2 complete.** Continuing the same request. Each player now has a play-chip wallet with a ledger: a starting grant, a daily top-up below 10,000, buy-ins and rebuys paid up front and refunded if refused, and cash-outs paid in the same transaction as the table save. Fixed in the table core on the way: a rebuy waiting for the hand to end vanished if the player left during that hand. A browser test (sit out, come back) failed now and then in Firefox at phone size; its trace showed the click landing where “Sit out next hand” had been a moment before the turn’s action panel opened and pushed it down. The test now waits for the panel. The jump itself is worth fixing in P6.5 or P6.6. Checks are in the P4.2 evidence. Not pushed or deployed. Next action: P4.3, the lobby (a table list, quick seat, private tables, waitlist), which also lets a second person sit at a table, something the wallet test could only try and have refunded.

**2026-11-19 · P4.1 complete.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible. Players are now anonymous rows with random handles, identified by a signed `HttpOnly` session cookie from `POST /api/session` instead of a bearer token, with origin checks on unsafe requests and the socket. The client starts a session on load and stores only the table id. Players who held a Phase 3 token keep their id and guest handle the first time the new page loads. Caddy’s rate limit moves from `/api/players` to `/api/session`, which every page load now calls (30 a minute per address). Pitfalls: Starlette’s test client opens sockets at `ws://testserver` whatever its `base_url`, so pass a full `wss://` URL to test origins; and psycopg’s `rowcount` is not reliable for `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so use `RETURNING`. Checks are in the P4.1 evidence. Not pushed or deployed. Next action: P4.2, the play-chip wallet and ledger.

## Phase 3 session handoffs

**2026-11-19 · P3.7, Phase 3 complete.** The owner decided Q-2 (recorded as D-11), approved pushing `main` and then approved the deploy. The push of the D-11 record failed CI on a test race: the restore test read the database before the save after a disconnect had finished. It now waits for the save, and with saves slowed by 50 ms the old test fails and the new one passes. Rehearsing `pnpm e2e:live` locally before deploying caught a turn ring widening the phone page. Its turn helper discarded the join snapshot, which was already the player’s turn, so it waited for the 50 s clock; it also always called, busted against random bots and waited for hands that `require_person` rightly never dealt. Next action: P3.7, once the owner approves the deploy. The cutover makes `/nl` the main page, runs `serve-nl` in the unit with `DATABASE_URL` from `web.env` and a secret file under `/var/lib/rivermind-poker`, and closes the open short-deck tables with a message.

## Phase 2 session handoffs

**2026-11-19 · P2.2 to P2.7, Phase 2 complete.** The owner asked for as much of the build as possible. Built the no-limit engine in `rivermind/engine`: an immutable state machine, pots and showdown, a faster port of Deuces, seating helpers, versioned JSON with replay, text histories, `SeatView` v2, a fuzzer (`python -m rivermind.engine.fuzz`) and a benchmark (`python -m rivermind.engine.bench`). The random, calling-station and heuristic bots are in `rivermind/bots`, and `hypothesis` is a new dev dependency. Rules changes for the owner to review: rule 7.8 (no bet or raise when everyone else still in is all-in) and rule 12.1 now also tables every hand when a river call puts a player all-in. The checks are in each step’s evidence; pytest 341 passed before the last commit. Nothing was pushed or deployed, and the web client was not changed, so its suite was not rerun. Next action: P3.1. PostgreSQL 17 runs locally on port 5432 for its tests, and creating the site’s database on ubuntu-vm needs the owner.

**2026-11-19 · P2.1.** Wrote [docs/rules.md](docs/rules.md), the no-limit specification for the Phase 2 engine. For the owner, the choices where card rooms differ: a moving button rather than a dead button; new and returning players dealt in at once with no missed-blind charges; an optional per-player ante; a short big blind still requires a full big blind to call; only a full raise in total reopens betting; odd chips go left of the button per pot; losing hands are auto-mucked and all-in hands tabled; burn cards are dealt; timeouts check or fold. Change any of them before P2.2 builds on them. Next action: P1.6 after the owner approves the deploy.
